\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi
is already globally Lipschitz,
[
33
, P. 128, Theorem 11.2]
guarantees that there is a unique solution
(\mathbf{u}^{N}_{m},c^{N}_{m},n^{N}_{m})
to a system associated to the system (
4.2
) with
\Psi_{m}^{N}
and
\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g^{N}
(instead of
\Psi_{m}
and
\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g
) and defined on
[0,+\infty)
almost surely. We then define a sequence of stopping times as follows
for all
m,N\in\mathbb{N}

(4.6)

\tau_{N}^{m}:=\inf\{t>0:\sqrt{\left|n^{N}_{m}(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\mathbf{u}^{N}_{m}(t)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left|c^{N}_{m}(t)\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}}\geq N\}\wedge N,

where
a\wedge b:=\min\{a,b\}
for any real numbers
a
and
b
.

For any fixed
m\in\mathbb{N}
, the sequence
\{\tau_{N}^{m}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is obviously increasing. Moreover
[
33
, P. 131, Corollary 11.10]
implies that for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

(\mathbf{u}_{m},c_{m},n_{m})=(\mathbf{u}^{N}_{m},c^{N}_{m},n^{N}_{m})\text{ on }[0,\tau_{N}^{m}].

From this last equality, we infer that the solution
(\mathbf{u}_{m},c_{m},n_{m})
of system (
4.2
) is defined on
[0,\tau_{N}^{m}]
for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
and hence,
\tau_{m}>\tau^{m}_{N}
almost surely for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
. Therefore,

\tau_{m}\geq\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\tau^{m}_{N},\ \mathbb{P}\text{-a.s}.

In order to prove the inequality (
4.5
), it is sufficient to prove that

(4.7)

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\tau^{m}_{N}>T,\ \mathbb{P}\text{-a.s}.

Before proving this, in the following lemma, we prove some properties of the local solution
(\mathbf{u}_{m},c_{m},n_{m})
of system (
4.2
).

Lemma 4.2
.

Assumption
2.1
and Assumption
2.2
. Then for all
m,N\in\mathbb{N}
, the following equality and inequalities hold
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

(4.8)

\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)dx=\int_{\mathcal{O}}n^{m}_{0}(x)dx,\text{ for all }t\in[0,T],

(4.9)

n_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})>0,\text{ and }c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})>0,\text{ for all }t\in[0,T],

and

(4.10)

\left|c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}},\text{ for all }t\in[0,T].

Proof.

In order to prove the non-negativity of
n_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})
and
c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})
, we will follow the idea of the proof of Lemma
3.6
. But, instead of the Gagliardo-Niremberg-Sobolev inequality, we will use the equivalence of the norms on finite dimensional space.

Let
N,m\in\mathbb{N}
and
t\in[0,T]
be arbitrary but fixed. For all
s\in[0,t]
define

n_{m_{-}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})}:=\max(-n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}),0).

We remark that
n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\in W^{2,2}(\mathcal{O})
and

\displaystyle n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})=0\cdot 1_{\{n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\geq 0\}}-n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\cdot 1_{\{n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})<0\}},

\displaystyle\nabla n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})=0\cdot 1_{\{n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\geq 0\}}-\nabla n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\cdot 1_{\{n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})<0\}},

\displaystyle\Delta n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})=0\cdot 1_{\{n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\geq 0\}}-\Delta n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\cdot 1_{\{n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})<0\}}.

We can easily see also that for all
s\in[0,t]
,

\displaystyle\frac{dn_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})}{dt}n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})=-\frac{dn_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})}{dt}n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}),

\displaystyle n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\nabla n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})=-n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\nabla n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}),

\displaystyle\Delta n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})=-\Delta n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}).

Hence, we multiply equation
(\ref{3.1})_{3}
by
n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})
for any
s\in[0,t]
, integrate over
\mathcal{O}
, and use an integration-by-parts with the fact that
\nabla\cdot\mathbf{u}_{m}=0
to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left|n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{u}_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)\cdot\nabla n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)dx-\delta\left|\nabla n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\chi\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)\nabla c_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)\nabla n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m_{-}}^{2}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)\nabla\cdot\mathbf{u}_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)dx-\delta\left|\nabla n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\chi\int_{\mathcal{\mathcal{O}}}n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)\nabla c_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)\nabla n_{-}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)dx

\displaystyle\leq-\delta\left|\nabla n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\chi\left|n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\nabla n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}\left|c_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|_{H^{2}}.

In the last line we have used the continuous embedding of
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})
into
L^{4}(\mathcal{O})
. Since the
L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
,
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})
and
H^{2}(\mathcal{O})
-norms are equivalent on
H_{m}
, we then infer from this last inequality that for all
s\in[0,t]
,

(4.11)

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left|n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\mathcal{K}(m)\left|n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left|c_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|_{L^{2}},

where
\mathcal{K}(m)
is a constant depending of
m
which is the dimension of the space
H_{m}
.
Owing to the fact that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. the paths of
c_{m}
are continuous, we derive that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left|c_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|_{L^{2}}<\infty,\ \ \mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

Hence, integrating (
4.11
) over
[0,t]
we arrive at

(4.12)

\left|n_{m_{-}}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\left|(n^{m}_{0})_{-}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{t}\left|c_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|_{L^{2}}\left|n_{m_{-}}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds.

Thanks to the Gronwall inequality, we derive from the inequality (
4.12
) that

\left|n_{m_{-}}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\left|(n^{m}_{0})_{-}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\exp\left(\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{t}\left|c_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|_{L^{2}}ds\right),

which implies that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s,
n_{m_{-}}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})=0
for all
t\in[0,T]
since by the relation (
4.1
),
n_{0}^{m}>0
.

The non-negativity property of
c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})
is quite similar to the proof of Lemma
3.6
. We consider the function
\Psi:H_{m}\to\mathbb{R}
defined by
\Psi(c)=\int_{\mathcal{O}}c_{-}^{2}(x)dx
where
c_{-}=\max(-c;0)
. Let
\{\psi_{h}\}_{h\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of smooth functions defined by
\psi_{h}(y)=y^{2}\varphi(hy)
, for all
y\in\mathbb{R}
and
h\in\mathbb{N}
, where the function
\varphi
is defined by (
3.8
).
We consider for any
h\geq 1
, the following sequence of function
\Psi_{h}:H_{m}\to\mathbb{R}
defined by
\Psi_{h}=\int_{\mathcal{O}}\psi_{h}(c(x))dx
, for
c\in H_{m}
. The mapping
\Psi_{h}
is twice (Fréchet) differentiable and its first and second derivatives are given by

\Psi^{\prime}_{h}(c)(z)=2\int_{\mathcal{O}}c(x)\varphi(hc(x))z(x)dx+h\int_{\mathcal{O}}c^{2}(x)\varphi^{\prime}(hc(x))z(x)dx,\quad\forall c,z\in H_{m},

and

\displaystyle\Psi_{h}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(c)(z,k)

\displaystyle=h^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}c^{2}(x)\varphi^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(hc(x))z(x)k(x)dx

\displaystyle\qquad{}+4h\int_{\mathcal{O}}c(x)\varphi^{\prime}(hc(x))z(x)k(x)dx+2\int_{\mathcal{O}}\varphi(hc(x))z(x)k(x)dx,\quad\forall c,z,k\in H_{m}.

Applying the Itô formula to
t\mapsto\Psi_{h}(c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}))
, we obtain for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\Psi_{h}(c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}))-\Psi_{h}(c_{m}(0))

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\Psi^{\prime}_{h}(c_{m}(s))\left(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\cdot\nabla c_{m}(s)+\xi\Delta c_{m}(s)-n_{m}(s)f(c_{m}(s))\right)ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\Psi^{{}^{\prime\prime}}_{h}(c_{m}(s))\left(\gamma\phi_{k}(c_{m}(s)),\gamma\phi_{k}(c_{m}(s))\right)ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\gamma\sum_{k=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\Psi^{\prime}_{h}(c_{m}(s))(\phi_{k}(c_{m}(s)))d\beta^{k}_{s}.

Similarly to (
3.10
), (
3.11
), (
3.12
), (
3.13
) and (
3.14
), we can infer from this last equality that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}}\psi_{h}(c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x))dx

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathcal{O}}\psi_{h}(c_{0}^{m}(x))dx

(4.13)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\Psi^{\prime}_{h}(c_{m}(s))\left(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\cdot\nabla c_{m}(s)+\eta\Delta c_{m}(s)-n_{m}(s)f(c_{m}(s))\right)ds.

Now, observe that from the assumptions on the function
\varphi
, we infer that for all
y\in\mathbb{R}
we have

(4.14)

\lim_{h\longrightarrow\infty}\psi_{h}(y)=-y^{2}\cdot 1_{\{y<0\}}=-y_{-}^{2}\ \text{ and }\ \lim_{h\longrightarrow\infty}2y\varphi(hy)=-2y\cdot 1_{\{y<0\}}.

We note that for any
y\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

(4.15)

\lim_{h\longrightarrow\infty}h\varphi^{\prime}(hy)=0,

and also that

(4.16)

\left|\psi_{h}(y)\right|\leq\mathcal{K}y^{2}\ \text{ and }\ \left|h\varphi^{\prime}(hy)\right|\leq\mathcal{K}\left|y\right|,

for any
y\in\mathbb{R}
and for all
h\geq 1
, where
\mathcal{K}>0
is a constant.

Using (
4.14
)-(
4.16
) and applying the Lebesgue Dominated Convergence Theorem, we can pass to the limit as
h
tends to infinity in (
4.13
).
In this way, we derive that

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathcal{O}}c^{2}_{m_{-}}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)dx+\int_{\mathcal{O}}(c_{0}^{m}(x))_{-}^{2}dx

\displaystyle=-2\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left((\mathbf{u}_{m}(s,x)\cdot\nabla c_{m}(s,x)+\eta\Delta c_{m}(s,x)))\right)c_{m}(s,x)1_{\{c_{m}(s,x)<0\}}dxds

(4.17)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(s,x)f(c_{m}(s,x))c_{m}(s,x)1_{\{c_{m}(s,x)<0\}}dxds

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\eta\left|\nabla c_{m}(s,x)\right|^{2}+n_{m}(s,x)f(c_{m}(s,x))c_{m}(s,x)\right)1_{\{c_{m}(s,x)<0\}}dxds,

where we have used integration-by-parts and the fact that
\nabla\cdot\mathbf{u}_{m}=0
.
By the mean value theorem we know that, for all
x\in\mathcal{O}
, there exists a number
\lambda_{m}(x)\in(\min(0,c_{m}(x)),\max(0,c_{m}(x)))
such that

f(c_{m}(x))-f(0)=c_{m}(x)f^{\prime}(\lambda_{m}(x)).

By the fact that
f(0)=0
, we infer from (
4.17
) that

\left|c_{m_{-}}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}-\left|(c_{0}^{m})_{-}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}=-2\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(s,x)f^{\prime}(\lambda_{m}(s,x))c_{m}^{2}(s,x)1_{\{c_{m}(s,x)<0\}}dxds.

Since
f^{\prime}>0
and
1_{\{c_{m}<0\}}>0
as well as on
[0,t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}]
,
c_{m}^{2}>0
and
n_{m}>0
, we deduce that
\left|c_{m_{-}}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\left|(c_{0}^{m})_{-}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}
. Owing to the fact that by the relation (
4.1
) we have
c^{m}_{0}>0
, we derive that
(c^{m}_{0})_{-}=0
and therefore
\left|c_{m_{-}}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}=0
. This gives
c_{m_{-}}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})=0
and implies that for all
t\in[0,T]
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s,
c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})>0
.

It remains to prove the inequality (
4.10
). The proof is similar to the proof of Corollary
3.7
. Let
p\geq 2
be an integer. Let
\Psi:H_{m}\to\mathbb{R}
be the functional defined by
\Psi(c)=\int_{\mathcal{O}}c^{p}(x)dx
. Note that the mapping
\Psi
is twice (Fréchet) differentiable and its first and second derivatives are given by

\displaystyle\Psi^{\prime}(c)(z)=p\int_{\mathcal{O}}c^{p-1}(x)z(x)dx,\quad\forall c,z\in H_{m},

\displaystyle\Psi^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(c)(z,k)=p(p-1)\int_{\mathcal{O}}c^{p-2}(x)z(x)k(x)dx,\quad\forall c,z,k\in H_{m}.

By applying the Itô formula to the process
t\mapsto\Psi(c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}))
, we derive that for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\Psi(c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}))-\Psi(c_{m}(0))

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\Psi^{\prime}(c_{m}(s))\left(\mathbf{u}(s)\cdot\nabla c_{m}(s)+\xi\Delta c_{m}(s)-n_{m}(s)G(c_{m}(s))\right)ds

\displaystyle\quad{}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\Psi^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(c_{m}(s))\left(\gamma\phi_{k}(c_{m}(s)),\gamma\phi_{k}(c_{m}(s))\right)ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\gamma\sum_{k=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\Psi^{\prime}(c_{m}(s))(\phi_{k}(c_{m}(s)))d\beta^{k}_{s},

from which and calculations similar to (
3.13
), (
3.18
), (
3.19
) and (
3.20
) we derive from the last equality that

\displaystyle\Psi(c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}))-\Psi(c_{0}^{m})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left(-p(p-2)\left|\nabla c_{m}(s,x)\right|^{2}c_{m}^{p-2}(s,x)-pn_{m}(s,x)f(c_{m}(s,x))c_{m}^{p-1}(s,x)\right)dxds.

Since for all
s\in[0,t]
the quantities
n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})
,
f(c_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}))
and
c_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})
are positive
\mathbb{P}
-a.s, we infer from the last equality that for all
t\in[0,T]
,
\Psi(c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}))\leq\Psi(c_{0}^{m}).

This implies that
\left|c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|_{L^{p}}\leq\left|c^{m}_{0}\right|_{L^{p}}
for all
p\geq 2
. Using the fact that
\left|.\right|_{L^{p}}\to\left|.\right|_{L^{\infty}}
as
p\to+\infty
and the inequality (
4.1
), we obtain the result.
∎

Next, we introduce for any
t\in[0,T]
and
m,N\in\mathbb{N}
, the following Lyapunov functional

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\ln n_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})dx+\mathcal{K}_{f}\left|\nabla c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\mathcal{K}_{4}}{\eta}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+e^{-1}\left|\mathcal{O}\right|,

where
\mathcal{K}_{4}
is some positive constant to be given later and
\mathcal{K}_{f}
is defined in (
2.2
).
Since
x\ln x\geq-e^{-1}
for any
x>0
, we can easily see that for all
t\in[0,T]
,
\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\geq 0
.
As in
[
44
]
the property (
4.1
) implies that

(4.18)

\mathcal{E}(n_{0}^{m},c_{0}^{m},\mathbf{u}_{0}^{m})\leq\mathcal{E}(n_{0},c_{0},\mathbf{u}_{0}),\qquad\text{for all }m\geq 1.

In addition, taking into account the inequality (
4.10
) and setting
\mathcal{K}=\min(\mathcal{K}_{f},\frac{\mathcal{K}_{4}}{\eta})
the following holds for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\left|(\mathbf{u}_{m}(t),c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}^{-1}\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})+\mathcal{K}^{-1}\left|c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(4.19)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}^{-1}\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})+\mathcal{K}^{-1}\left|\mathcal{O}\right|\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2},\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

We now proceed to establish some uniform bounds for
\mathbf{u}_{m}
,
c_{m}
, and
n_{m}
in some suitable spaces. For this purpose, we recall that hereafter,
\mathcal{K}
will denote a positive constant independent of
m
and
N
, which may change from one term to the next.

Lemma 4.3
.

Under the same assumptions as in Proposition
3.5
, there exists a positive constant
\mathcal{K}
such that for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

(4.20)

\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|c_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\eta\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\leq\left|\mathcal{O}\right|\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}},\qquad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(4.21)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\leq\mathcal{K},\\
&\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left(\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)ds\leq\mathcal{K}.\end{split}

Proof.

Let
t\in[0,T]
be arbitrary but fixed. We start by proving the estimate (
4.20
). To do this, we take
(m,N)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
arbitrary and apply the Itô formula to
t\mapsto\left|c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}
to get

\displaystyle\left|c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\xi\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

(4.22)

\displaystyle=\left|c^{m}_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}-2\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}(B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s)),c_{m}(s))ds-2\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}(R_{1}(n_{m}(s),c_{m}(s)),c_{m}(s))ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\gamma^{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\phi(c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}+2\gamma\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}(\phi(c_{m}(s)),c_{m}(s))d\beta_{s}.

By integration by part, we derive that

(B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m},c_{m}),c_{m})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{u}_{m}(x)\cdot\nabla c_{m}^{2}(x)dx=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}c_{m}^{2}(x)\nabla\cdot\mathbf{u}_{m}(x)dx=0.

By the free divergence property of
\sigma_{k}
and the fact that
\sigma_{k}=0
on
\partial\mathcal{O}
,
k=1,2
, we get

\displaystyle(\phi(c_{m}),c_{m})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\sigma_{k}(x)\cdot\nabla c_{m}(x)c_{m}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\sigma_{k}(x)\cdot\nabla c_{m}^{2}(x)dx

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}c_{m}^{2}(x)\nabla\cdot\sigma_{k}(x)dx+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}c_{m}^{2}(\sigma)\sigma_{k}(\sigma)\cdot\nu d\sigma

\displaystyle=0.

Taking into account the equality (
3.13
), we infer that

\left|\phi(c_{m})\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}=\sum_{k=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left|\sigma_{k}(x)\cdot\nabla c_{m}(x)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}dx=\left|\nabla c_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Using these three last equalities and the fact that
\left|c^{m}_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\left|\mathcal{O}\right|\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}
(since by the relation (
4.1
),
\left|c^{m}_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\leq\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}
), we infer from the equality (
4.22
) that for all
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.23)

\left|c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\eta\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+2\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(s,x)f(c_{m}(s,x))c_{m}(s,x)dxds\leq\left|\mathcal{O}\right|\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}.

Thanks to the non-negativity of
n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})
,
c_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})
and
f
over the interval
[0,t]
given in Lemma
4.2
and Assumption
2.1
, we can deduce from the inequality (
4.23
) that

(4.24)

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\eta\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\leq\left|\mathcal{O}\right|\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}},\qquad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

Let us now move to the proof of the estimate (
4.21
).

Multiplying equation (
2.14
)
3
by
1+\ln n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})
for
s\in[0,t]
and integrate the resulting equation in
\mathcal{O}
and using an integration-by-parts as well as the divergence free property of
\mathbf{u}_{m}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)\ln n(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)dx+\delta\int_{\mathcal{O}}\dfrac{\left|\nabla n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)\right|^{2}}{n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)}dx

(4.25)

\displaystyle=\chi\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla n_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)\cdot\nabla c_{m}(s\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)dx.

In the equality (
4.1
), we have used the fact that
\mathbf{u}_{m}=\frac{\partial n_{m}}{\partial\nu}=0
on
\partial\mathcal{O}
and the fact that

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathcal{O}}\Delta n_{m}(x)\ln(n_{m}(x))dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla n_{m}(x)\cdot\nabla\ln(n_{m}(x))dx-\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}\frac{\partial n_{m}(\sigma)}{\partial\nu}\ln(n_{m}(\sigma))d\sigma

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{O}}\frac{\nabla n_{m}(x)\cdot\nabla n_{m}(x)}{n_{m}(x)}dx,

as well as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{u}_{m}(x)\cdot\nabla n_{m}(x)\ln(n_{m}(x))dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(x)\nabla\cdot(\mathbf{u}_{m}(x)\ln(n_{m}(x)))dx

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(\sigma)\ln(n_{m}(\sigma))\mathbf{u}_{m}(\sigma)\cdot\nu d\sigma

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(x)\mathbf{u}_{m}(x)\cdot\nabla\ln(n_{m}(x))dx

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(x)\ln(n_{m}(x))\nabla\cdot\mathbf{u}_{m}(x)dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{u}_{m}(x)\cdot\nabla n_{m}(x)dx.

It follows from the Young inequality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that

\chi\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla n_{m}(x)\cdot\nabla c_{m}(x)dx\leq\frac{\delta}{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\dfrac{\left|\nabla n_{m}(x)\right|^{2}}{n_{m}(x)}dx+\frac{\chi^{2}}{2\delta}\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(x)\left|\nabla c_{m}(x)\right|^{2}dx.

Since

\int_{\mathcal{O}}\dfrac{\left|\nabla n_{m}(x)\right|^{2}}{n_{m}(x)}dx=4\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(x)}\right|^{2}dx,

we may combine the last inequality with equality (
4.1
) to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)\ln n_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)dx+2\delta\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

(4.26)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{O}}n^{m}_{0}(x)\ln n^{m}_{0}(x)dx+\frac{\chi^{2}}{2\delta}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds.

Applying the Itô formula once more to
t\mapsto\left|\nabla c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}
, yields

\displaystyle\left|\nabla c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\xi\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle=\left|\nabla c^{m}_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}-2\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}(\nabla B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s)),\nabla c_{m}(s))ds

(4.27)

\displaystyle\qquad{}-2\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}(\nabla R_{1}(n_{m}(s),c_{m}(s)),\nabla c_{m}(s))ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\gamma^{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}+2\gamma\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}(\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s)),\nabla c_{m}(s))d\beta_{s}.

Since
\mathbf{u}_{m}
is solenoidal and vanishes on
\partial\mathcal{O}
, we derive that

\displaystyle(\nabla B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m},c_{m}),\nabla c_{m})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla(\mathbf{u}_{m}(x)\cdot\nabla c_{m}(x))\cdot\nabla c_{m}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(x)\nabla c_{m}(x)\cdot\nabla c_{m}(x)dx

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla c_{m}(x)\cdot D^{2}c_{m}(x)\mathbf{u}_{m}(x)dx

(4.28)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(x)\right|\left|\nabla c_{m}(x)\right|^{2}dx+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}u_{m}(x)\cdot\nabla\left|\nabla c_{m}(x)\right|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla c_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{4}}.

We use the Gagliardo-Niremberg inequality to obtain

\left|\nabla c_{m}\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}\leq\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|D^{2}c_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{m}\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{4},

To cancel
\left|D^{2}c_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}
, we invoke the pointwise identity

\left|\Delta c_{m}\right|^{2}=\nabla\cdot(\Delta c_{m}\nabla c_{m})-\nabla c_{m}\cdot\nabla\Delta c_{m},

and
\Delta\left|\nabla c_{m}\right|^{2}=2\nabla c_{m}\cdot\nabla\Delta c_{m}+2\left|D^{2}c_{m}\right|^{2}
, as well as the integration-by-parts to rewrite
\left|\Delta c_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}
as

\displaystyle\left|\Delta c_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla c_{m}(x)\cdot\nabla\Delta c_{m}(x)dx

(4.29)

\displaystyle=\left|D^{2}c_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\Delta\left|\nabla c_{m}(x)\right|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\left|D^{2}c_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}\frac{\partial\left|\nabla c_{m}(\sigma)\right|^{2}}{\partial\nu}d\sigma.

Invoking
[
27
, Lemma 4.2]
we obtain

(4.30)

\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}\frac{\partial\left|\nabla c_{m}(\sigma)\right|^{2}}{\partial\nu}d\sigma\leq\kappa(\mathcal{O})\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(\sigma)\right|^{2}d\sigma,

where
\kappa(\mathcal{O})
is an upper bound for the curvatures of
\partial\mathcal{O}
.

Thanks to the trace theorem (see
[
21
, (ii) of Proposition 4.22 with (i) of Theorem 4.24]
), it holds that

\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(\sigma)\right|^{2}d\sigma\leq\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{O},\varsigma)\left|c_{m}\right|^{2}_{H^{\frac{3+\varsigma}{2}}}\qquad\text{for any }\varsigma\in(0,1),

where
\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{O},\varsigma)>0
depends only on
\mathcal{O}
and
\varsigma
, which can be fixed for instance
\varsigma=1/2
. On the other hand, the interpolation inequality, the Young inequality and the inequality (
4.10
) of Lemma
4.2
imply the existence of
\mathcal{K}_{1}
and
\mathcal{K}_{2}
depending on
\mathcal{O}
such that

\displaystyle\kappa(\mathcal{O})\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{O},\varsigma)\left|c_{m}\right|^{2}_{H^{\frac{7}{4}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}_{1}(\left|D^{2}c_{m}\right|^{7/4}_{L^{2}}\left|c_{m}\right|^{1/4}_{L^{2}}+\left|c_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\left|D^{2}c_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K}_{2}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}.

Using this previous inequality and (
4.30
), we infer from the equality (
4.29
) that

(4.31)

\left|D^{2}c_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\frac{4}{3}\left|\Delta c_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{4\mathcal{K}_{2}}{3}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}},

and therefore

\left|\nabla c_{m}\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}\leq\frac{4\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3}\left|\Delta c_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left(\frac{4\mathcal{K}_{2}}{3}+1\right)\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}.

By the inequality (
4.28
) and the Young inequality, we infer that

\displaystyle(\nabla B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m},c_{m}),\nabla c_{m})

\displaystyle\leq\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla c_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\xi}{16\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}\left|\nabla c_{m}\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\frac{4\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\xi}{4}\left|\Delta c_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{4\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\xi(4\mathcal{K}_{2}+3)}{16}\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}.

Due to the Assumption
1
and the inequality (
4.10
) of Lemma
4.2
, we note that

\displaystyle-(\nabla R_{1}(n_{m},c_{m}),\nabla c_{m})

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla(n_{m}(x)f(c_{m}(x)))\cdot\nabla c_{m}(x)dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathcal{O}}f^{\prime}(c_{m}(x))\left|\nabla c_{m}(x)\right|^{2}n_{m}(x)dx-\int_{\mathcal{O}}f(c_{m}(x))\nabla c_{m}(x)\cdot\nabla n_{m}(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\displaystyle\min_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{\prime}(c)}{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(x)\left|\nabla c_{m}(x)\right|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{1}{2\displaystyle\min_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{\prime}(c)}\int_{\mathcal{O}}f^{2}(c_{m}(x))\frac{\left|\nabla n_{m}(x)\right|^{2}}{n_{m}(x)}dx

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\displaystyle\min_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{\prime}(c)}{2}\left|\sqrt{n_{m}}\nabla c_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{2\displaystyle\max_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{2}(c)}{\displaystyle\min_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{\prime}(c)}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Combining these two last inequalities, we derive from equality (
4.27
) that

\displaystyle\left|\nabla c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{3\xi}{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\displaystyle\min_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{\prime}(c)\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\left|\nabla c^{m}_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\xi(4\mathcal{K}_{2}+3)}{8}\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}t+\frac{8\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{4\displaystyle\max_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{2}(c)}{\displaystyle\min_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{\prime}(c)}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\gamma^{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}ds+2\gamma\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}(\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s)),\nabla c_{m}(s))d\beta_{s}.

Multiplying this last inequality by
\mathcal{K}_{f}
and adding the result with inequality (
4.1
) we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)\ln n_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)dx+\mathcal{K}_{f}\left|\nabla c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\xi\mathcal{K}_{f}}{4}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+2\delta\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}_{f}\left|\nabla c^{m}_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{\mathcal{O}}n^{m}_{0}(x)\ln n^{m}_{0}(x)dx+\frac{\mathcal{K}_{f}\xi(4\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathcal{K}_{2}+3)}{8}\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}t

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{8\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\frac{4\mathcal{K}_{f}\displaystyle\max_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{2}(c)}{\displaystyle\min_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{\prime}(c)}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\gamma^{2}\mathcal{K}_{f}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}ds+2\gamma\mathcal{K}_{f}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}(\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s)),\nabla c_{m}(s))d\beta_{s}.

By using the first inequality of (
3.3
), we see that the previous inequality reduces to

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)\ln n_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m},x)dx+\mathcal{K}_{f}\left|\nabla c_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{3\xi\mathcal{K}_{f}}{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+2\delta\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}_{f}\left|\nabla c^{m}_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{\mathcal{O}}n^{m}_{0}(x)\ln n^{m}_{0}(x)dx+\frac{\mathcal{K}_{f}\xi(4\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathcal{K}_{2}+3)}{8}\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}t

(4.32)

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{8\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\gamma^{2}\mathcal{K}_{f}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+2\gamma\mathcal{K}_{f}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}(\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s)),\nabla c_{m}(s))d\beta_{s}.

Now, we use the equality (
4.8
) of Lemma
4.2
and the inequality (
3.7
) to obtain that

\displaystyle\left|n_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left(\left|\sqrt{n_{m}}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}}\right|_{L^{2}}+\left|\sqrt{n_{m}}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)

(4.33)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left(\left|n^{m}_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}}\right|_{L^{2}}+\left|n^{m}_{0}\right|_{L^{1}}\right),

By the relation (
4.1
), we have
n_{0}^{m}\to n_{0}\text{ in }L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
. Thanks to the continuous embedding of
L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
into
L^{1}(\mathcal{O})
, we derive that
n_{0}^{m}\to n_{0}\text{ in }L^{1}(\mathcal{O})
and therefore the sequence
\{n_{0}^{m}\}_{m\geq 1}
is bounded in
L^{1}(\mathcal{O})
. This implies that the inequality (
4.33
) can be controlled as follows

(4.34)

\displaystyle\left|n_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}\leq\mathcal{K}_{GN}\mathcal{K}^{1/2}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}}\right|_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K},

where
\mathcal{K}
is a constant independent of
m
and
N
.

Next, applying the Itô formula to
t\mapsto\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}
and using the estimation (
4.34
), we infer the existence of
\mathcal{K}_{3}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\eta\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\Phi\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U};H)}ds+2\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}(g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s)),\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))dWs

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mathbf{u}^{m}_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\delta\eta}{\mathcal{K}_{4}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds+\mathcal{K}_{3}\left|\nabla\Phi\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{1}{2}t+\frac{1}{2}\left|\nabla\Phi\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\mathcal{K}^{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U};H)}ds+2\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}(g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s)),\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))dWs,

with
\mathcal{K}_{4}=\frac{8\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi}
. Multiplying this inequality by
\frac{\mathcal{K}_{4}}{\eta}
, and adding the result with inequality (
4.32
) after using the inequality (
4.18
), we see that there exists positive constants
\mathcal{K}_{5}
and
\mathcal{K}_{6}
such that for all
t\in[0,T]
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})+\delta\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left[\frac{3\xi\mathcal{K}_{f}}{2}\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K}_{4}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]ds

(4.35)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{E}(n_{0},c_{0},\mathbf{u}_{0})+\mathcal{K}_{5}T+\mathcal{K}_{6}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\gamma^{2}\mathcal{K}_{f}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{2\mathcal{K}_{4}}{\eta}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}(g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s)),\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))dW_{s}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{\mathcal{K}_{4}}{\eta}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}\left|g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U},H)}ds+2\gamma\mathcal{K}_{f}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m}}(\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s)),\nabla c_{m}(s))d\beta_{s}.

Now, since
\gamma
satisfies the relation (
3.3
), taking into account the inequality (
4.31
), we note that

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}\mathcal{K}_{f}\left|\nabla\phi(c_{m})\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\gamma^{2}\mathcal{K}_{f}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left|\nabla\sigma_{k}(x)\nabla c(x)\right|^{2}dx+2\gamma^{2}\mathcal{K}_{f}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left|D^{2}c(x)\sigma_{k}(x)\right|^{2}dx

(4.36)

\displaystyle\leq 2\gamma^{2}\mathcal{K}_{f}\left|\nabla c\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\left|\sigma_{k}\right|^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}}+\left|\Delta c\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\frac{8\gamma^{2}\mathcal{K}_{f}}{3}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\left|\sigma_{k}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+\frac{8\gamma^{2}\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathcal{K}_{2}}{3}\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\left|\sigma_{k}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|\nabla c\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\xi\mathcal{K}_{f}}{2}\left|\Delta c\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K}.

By the inequalities (
2.12
) and (
4.19
), we also note that

\displaystyle\left|g(\mathbf{u}_{m},c_{m})\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U},H)}

\displaystyle\leq 2L^{2}_{g}\left|(\mathbf{u}_{m},c_{m})\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}}+2L^{2}_{g}

(4.37)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})+\mathcal{K}\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+2L^{2}_{g}.

From the estimates (
4.35
) until (
4.37
), we derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})+\delta\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left[\xi\mathcal{K}_{f}\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K}_{4}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{E}(n_{0},c_{0},\mathbf{u}_{0})+\mathcal{K}T+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\mathcal{E}(n_{m}(s),c_{m}(s),\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))ds+2L^{2}_{g}T

(4.38)

\displaystyle\qquad{}+2\gamma\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\int_{0}^{s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}(\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s)),\nabla c_{m}(s))d\beta_{s}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{2\mathcal{K}_{4}}{\eta}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}(g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))e_{k},\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))dW^{k}_{s}\right|.

Now, by making use of the Burholder-Davis-Gundy, Cauchy-Schwarz, Young inequalities and the fact that
\gamma
satisfies the relation (
3.3
), we infer that

\displaystyle 2\gamma\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\int_{0}^{s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}(\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s)),\nabla c_{m}(s))d\beta_{s}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|(\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s)),\nabla c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{K}_{f}}{4}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathcal{E}(n_{m}(s),c_{m}(s),\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{\xi\mathcal{K}_{f}}{2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\mathcal{K}T.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\frac{2\mathcal{K}_{4}}{\eta}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}(g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))e_{k},\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))dW^{k}_{s}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U};H)}ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}}ds+\mathcal{K}TL_{g}^{2}.

It follows from the estimates (
4.38
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})

(4.39)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left[\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K}_{f}\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathcal{E}(n_{0},c_{0},\mathbf{u}_{0})+\mathcal{K}T+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s)ds+\mathcal{K},

where
\mathcal{K}
is a constant depending on the initial data and
T
but independent of
m
and
N
.
Now, the Gronwall lemma yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left[\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]ds\leq\mathcal{K},

from which we deduce the estimates (
4.21
) and hence completing the proof of Lemma
4.3
.
∎

Lemma 4.4
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
4.3
, for all
p\geq 1
, there exists a positive constant
\mathcal{K}
such that we have for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

(4.40)

\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{2p}_{L^{2}}+\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{p}\leq\left|\mathcal{O}\right|^{p}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2p}_{L^{\infty}},\qquad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.},\\

(4.41)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathcal{E}^{p}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathcal{K},\\
&\text{and }\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathcal{K}.\end{split}

Proof.

The inequality (
4.40
) follows directly from the estimates (
4.20
) of Lemma
4.3
. Next, we are going to derive estimate (
4.41
). We start with the inequality (
4.38
) and invoke the Jensen inequality to derive that for all
p\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathcal{E}^{p}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\xi\mathcal{K}_{f}\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\mathcal{K}_{4}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}

(4.42)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{E}^{p}(n_{0},c_{0},\mathbf{u}_{0})+\mathcal{K}T^{p}+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s)ds\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\mathcal{K}^{p}+2^{p}\gamma^{p}\mathcal{K}_{f}^{p}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\int_{0}^{s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}(\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s)),\nabla c_{m}(s))d\beta_{s}\right|^{p}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}(g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))e_{k},\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))dW^{k}_{s}\right|^{p}.

Invoking the Hölder inequality, we see that

\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\mathcal{E}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s)ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathcal{K}T^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\mathcal{E}^{p}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s)ds.

Thanks to the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality, we see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}(g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))e_{k},\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))dW^{k}_{s}\right|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|(g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))e_{k},\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))\right|^{2}ds\right)^{p/2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|u_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{p}_{L^{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U};H)}ds\right)^{p/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathcal{E}^{p}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U};H)}ds\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathcal{E}^{p}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2p}_{\mathcal{H}}ds+\mathcal{K}T^{\frac{p}{p-1}}L_{g}^{2p}.

Taking into account the fact that
\gamma
is sufficiently small such that the relation (
3.3
) is satisfied, we also arrive at

\displaystyle 2^{p}\gamma^{p}\mathcal{K}_{f}^{p}\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\int_{0}^{s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}(\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s)),\nabla c_{m}(s))d\beta_{s}\right|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p}\gamma^{p}\mathcal{K}_{f}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{p/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{2}}^{2p}+2^{2p}\gamma^{2p}\mathcal{K}_{f}^{2p}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla\phi(c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}ds\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathcal{E}^{p}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\xi\mathcal{K}_{f}\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}+\mathcal{K}T^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{2p}_{L^{2}}ds+\mathcal{K}T^{p}.

It follows from the estimates (
4.42
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathcal{E}^{p}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathcal{E}^{p}(n_{0},c_{0},\mathbf{u}_{0})+\mathcal{K}T^{p}+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\mathcal{E}^{p}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s)ds+\mathcal{K}.

Now, the Gronwall lemma yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathcal{E}^{p}(n_{m},c_{m},\mathbf{u}_{m})(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathcal{K},

and the estimate (
4.41
) follows directly from this last inequality. This completes the proof of Lemma
4.4
.
∎

In order to control the process
t\mapsto n_{m}(t\wedge\tau_{N}^{m})
, we prove the following lemma.

Lemma 4.5
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
4.3
, there exists a positive constant
\eta_{0}>1
such that for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}
,

(4.43)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}ds\leq\eta_{0}\exp\left(\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}^{4}ds\right).

Proof.

Let
t\in[0,T]
be arbitrary but fixed. Multiplying the last equation of (
4.2
) by
n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})
for
0\leq s\leq t
, and using the fact that
\nabla\cdot\mathbf{u}_{m}=0
and the inequality (
3.7
) as well as the Hölder inequality and the Young inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left|n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\delta\left|\nabla n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\xi\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N},x)\nabla c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N},x)\cdot\nabla n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N},x)dx

\displaystyle\leq\xi\left|n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\nabla n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}(\left|n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{1/2}_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{1/2}_{L^{2}}+\left|n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{2}})\left|\nabla c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\nabla n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{1/2}_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\nabla n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{3/2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{K}\left|n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\nabla n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{2}\left|\nabla n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K}\left|n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}(\left|\nabla c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left|\nabla c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{4}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{2}\left|\nabla n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K}\left|n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\left(\left|\nabla c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}+1\right).

This implies that for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left|n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\delta\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla n_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\leq\left|n^{m}_{0}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left(\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}+1\right)ds.

Since
n_{m}^{0}\to n_{0}
in
L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
,
\left|n_{0}^{m}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}
is uniformly bounded. Thus, applying the Gronwall lemma, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left|n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}_{\delta}\exp\left(\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left(\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}+1\right)ds\right)

\displaystyle\leq(\mathcal{K}_{\delta}+1)e^{\mathcal{K}T}\exp\left(\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}ds\right),

and complete the proof of Lemma
4.5
.
∎

Corollary 4.6
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
4.3
, for any
p\geq 1
, there exists a positive constant
\mathcal{K}
such that for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

(4.44)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{2p}_{L^{2}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathcal{K},

(4.45)

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathcal{K},

(4.46)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{2p}_{H^{1}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|c_{m}(s)\right|_{H^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathcal{K},

(4.47)

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{4}_{L^{2}}ds\leq\mathcal{K}.

Proof.

The estimate (
4.44
) is a consequence of the estimates (
4.21
) and (
4.41
). From the inequalities (
4.34
), (
4.21
) and (
4.41
), we infer that

\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left(\mathcal{K}_{GN}\mathcal{K}^{1/2}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n_{m}(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K}\right)ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathcal{K},

which proves the second estimate of inequality (
4.45
).

According to
[
35
, Proposition 7.2, P. 404]
, we have

\left|c_{m}\right|^{2}_{H^{2}}\leq\mathcal{K}(\left|\Delta c_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|c_{m}\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}),

from which along with (
4.21
) and (
4.41
) we deduce (
4.46
).

By applying the inequality (
3.7
), we obtain that

\left|\nabla c_{m}\right|_{L^{4}}^{4}\leq\mathcal{K}(\left|c_{m}\right|_{H^{2}}^{2}\left|\nabla c_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\nabla c_{m}\right|^{4}_{L^{2}}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}^{4}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|c_{m}(s)\right|_{H^{2}}^{2}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{4}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{2}_{H^{1}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{m}(s)\right|_{H^{2}}^{2}ds+\mathcal{K}T\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{4}_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{4}_{H^{1}}+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|c_{m}(s)\right|_{H^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{2},

from which along with (
4.46
) we deduce (
4.47
). This completes the proof of Corollary
4.6
.
∎

In the following lemma, we state and prove a result concerning the stopping time
\tau^{m}_{N}
. More precisely, we prove that
\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\tau_{m}^{N}\geq 2T
with probability
1
such that the inequality (
4.7
) holds.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\tau^{m}_{N}
,
m,N\in\mathbb{N}
be the stopping times defined in (
4.6
). Then, under the same assumptions as in Lemma
4.3
, it holds that

(4.48)

\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\tau_{m}^{N}(\omega)\geq 2T\right\}=1.

Consequently, the solutions
(\mathbf{u}_{m},c_{m},n_{m})
of system (
4.2
) exist almost surely for every
t\in[0,T]
.

Proof.

We notice that the inequalities of Corollary
4.6
hold for every
T>0
. Hence, for a fixed
T>0
, we set
\tilde{T}=2T
and note that for all
J\in\mathbb{N}
,

\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\tau_{m}^{N}(\omega)<\tilde{T}\right\}\subset\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\tau_{m}^{J}(\omega)<\tilde{T}\right\},

which implies that

(4.49)

\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\tau_{m}^{N}(\omega)<2T\right\}\leq\lim_{N\longrightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\tau_{m}^{N}(\omega)<\tilde{T}\right\},

and therefore, it is enough to show that the second term of the right hand side of this last equality converges to zero as
N\to\infty
. To this end, let

A_{N}=\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\tau_{m}^{N}<\tilde{T}\right\}

and

B_{N}=\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\left|n_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left|c_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}\geq N^{2}\right\}.

Then, we have
A_{N}\subset B_{N}
for
N>\tilde{T}
. Indeed, let
\omega\in A_{N}
, then
\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N}(\omega)=\tau_{m}^{N}(\omega)
. Thus, by the definition of the stopping time
\tau_{m}^{N}
, we see that for
N>\tilde{T}
,

\displaystyle\left|n_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left|c_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|n_{m}(\tau_{m}^{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\tau_{m}^{N})\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left|c_{m}(\tau_{m}^{N})\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq N^{2}.

We then conclude that
\omega\in B_{N}
.

Now, for
N>\tilde{T}
, using the inclusion
A_{N}\subset B_{N}
we derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\tau_{m}^{N}<\tilde{T}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\left|n_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left|c_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}\geq N^{2}\right\}

(4.50)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\left|n_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\geq\frac{N^{2}}{3}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq\frac{N^{2}}{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\left|c_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}\geq\frac{N^{2}}{3}\right\}.

According to the estimates (
4.44
) and (
4.46
) of Corollary
4.6
as well as the Markov inequality, we derive that for
N>\tilde{T}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\left|c_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}\geq\frac{N^{2}}{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\sup_{0\leq s\leq{\tilde{T}}}\left|c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}\geq\frac{N^{2}}{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{\tilde{T}}}\left|c_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{K}}{N^{2}},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq\frac{N^{2}}{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\sup_{0\leq s\leq{\tilde{T}}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq\frac{N^{2}}{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{\tilde{T}}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{K}}{N^{2}}.

Also for
N>\max(\sqrt{3\eta_{0}},\tilde{T})
(where
\eta_{0}
is a constant obtained in Lemma
4.5
), we use the inequality (
4.43
) of Lemma
4.5
to infer that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\left|n_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\geq\frac{N^{2}}{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\sup_{0\leq s\leq{\tilde{T}}}\left|n_{m}(s\wedge\tau^{m}_{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\geq\frac{N^{2}}{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\eta_{0}\exp\left(\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{\tilde{T}\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}^{4}ds\right)\geq\frac{N^{2}}{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\int_{0}^{\tilde{T}\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}^{4}ds\geq\frac{\ln(\frac{N^{2}}{3\eta_{0}})}{\mathcal{K}}\right\}.

Invoking the Markov inequality and using the estimate (
4.47
) of Corollary
4.6
, we see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\left|n_{m}(\tilde{T}\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\geq\frac{N^{2}}{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{K}}{\ln(\frac{N^{2}}{3\eta_{0}})}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{\tilde{T}\wedge\tau^{m}_{N}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}^{4}ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{K}}{2\ln(N)-\ln(3\eta_{0})}.

Plugging these inequalities into the inequality (
4.50
), we arrive at

\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\tau_{m}^{N}<\tilde{T}\right\}\leq\frac{\mathcal{K}}{N^{2}}+\frac{\mathcal{K}}{2\ln(N)-\ln(\eta_{0})-\ln(3)},

for all for
N>\max(\sqrt{3\eta_{0}},\tilde{T})
. Letting
N
to infinity in this last inequality we get

\lim_{N\longrightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\tau_{m}^{N}<\tilde{T}\right\}=0,

which along with (
4.49
) imply (
4.48
).

By the equality (
4.48
) we infer the inequality (
4.7
) and therefore, the relation (
4.5
) hold
and the lemma is then proved.
∎

Since
(T\wedge\tau_{m}^{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is increasing, we have
T\wedge\tau_{m}^{N}\to T
a.s., as
N\to\infty
.
With this almost surely convergence in hand, we are going to give some consequences of Lemma
4.5
and Corollary
4.6
.

Corollary 4.8
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
4.3
, for any
p\geq 1
, there exists a positive constant
\mathcal{K}
such that for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

(4.51)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}ds\leq\eta_{0}\exp\left(\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}^{4}ds\right),\ \mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(4.52)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2p}_{L^{2}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathcal{K},

(4.53)

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathcal{K},

(4.54)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|c_{m}(s)\right|^{2p}_{H^{1}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{m}(s)\right|_{H^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathcal{K},

(4.55)

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{4}_{L^{2}}ds\leq\mathcal{K},

where
\eta_{0}>1
is a constant obtained in Lemma
4.5
.

Proof.

Since
T\wedge\tau_{m}^{N}\to T
a.s., as
N\to\infty
, by the
path continuity of the process
t\mapsto(\mathbf{u}_{m}(t),c_{m}(t),n_{m}(t))
, we can let
N\to\infty
in the inequality (
4.43
) of Lemma
4.5
and derive the inequality (
4.51
). In addition to the almost surely convergence of
T\wedge\tau_{m}^{N}
to
T
and the
path continuity of the process
t\mapsto(\mathbf{u}_{m}(t),c_{m}(t),n_{m}(t))
, we invoke the Fatou lemma and pass to the limit as
N\to\infty
in the inequalities (
4.44
), (
4.45
), (
4.46
) and (
4.47
) and derive the estimate (
4.52
), (
4.53
), (
4.54
) and (
4.55
).
∎

Corollary 4.9
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
4.3
, there exists a positive constant
\mathcal{K}
such that for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

(4.56)

\mathbb{E}\left|n_{m}\right|^{2}_{C^{1/2}([0,T];H^{-3})}\leq\mathcal{K}.

Proof.

Let
v\in H^{3}(\mathcal{O})
. We recall that
\left|\nabla v\right|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\mathcal{K}\left|v\right|_{H^{3}}
. So, using an integration by part and the Hölder inequality, we derive that

\left|(A_{1}n_{m},v)\right|=\left|(n_{m},\Delta v)\right|\leq\left|n_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\Delta v\right|_{L^{2}}\leq\left|n_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|v\right|_{H^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m},n_{m}),v)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|(B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m},n_{m}),\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}v)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|(n_{m}\mathbf{u}_{m},\nabla\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}v)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|n_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}v\right|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|n_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|v\right|_{H^{3}},

and

\displaystyle\left|(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}R_{2}(n_{m},c_{m}),v)\right|

\displaystyle=\xi\left|(n_{m}\nabla c_{m},\nabla\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}v)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|n_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla c_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}v\right|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|n_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla c_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|v\right|_{H^{3}}.

Due to the continuous Sobolev embeddings
W^{1,2}(0,T;H^{-3}(\mathcal{O}))\hookrightarrow C^{1/2}(0,T;H^{-3}(\mathcal{O}))
, and
L^{2}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow H^{-3}(\mathcal{O})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|n_{m}\right|^{2}_{C^{1/2}(0,T;H^{-3})}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left|n_{m}\right|^{2}_{W^{1,2}(0,T;H^{-3})}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{-3}}ds+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\frac{d}{dt}n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{-3}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\frac{d}{dt}n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{-3}}ds.

Using the estimates (
4.52
), (
4.53
) and (
4.54
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|n_{m}\right|^{2}_{C^{1/2}(0,T;H^{-3}))}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|A_{1}n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{-3}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left[\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),n_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{H^{-3}}+\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}R_{2}(n_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{H^{-3}}\right]ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left[\left|n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|u_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right]ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{4}_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{4}_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{2}\leq\mathcal{K}.

∎

Lemma 4.10
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
4.3
, there exists a positive constant
\mathcal{K}
such that for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

(4.57)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left[\left|A_{1}c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{1}(n_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]ds\leq\mathcal{K},\\
&\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left[\left|A_{0}\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|_{V^{*}}^{2}+\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{0}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{V^{*}}+\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{0}(n_{m}(s),\varPhi)\right|_{V^{*}}^{2}\right]ds\leq\mathcal{K}.\end{split}

Proof.

Thanks to the inequalities (
4.52
), (
4.53
) and (
4.54
) once more, we note that

\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|A_{1}c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds=\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\Delta c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}}ds\leq\mathcal{K},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\cdot\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|u_{m}(s)\right|^{4}_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{2}\leq\mathcal{K},

as well as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{1}(n_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{m}(s)f(c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\sup_{0\leq s\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{2}(s)\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\mathcal{K},

and

\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|A_{0}\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|_{V^{*}}^{2}ds\leq\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\leq\mathcal{K}.

In the same way,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{0}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))\right|_{V^{*}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|u_{m}(s)\right|^{4}_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{2}\leq\mathcal{K},

and

\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|R_{0}(n_{m}(s),\varPhi)\right|_{V^{*}}^{2}ds\leq\left|\varPhi\right|^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\leq\mathcal{K}.

Combining all these inequalities, we obtain the relation (
4.57
).
∎

4.2.
Tightness result and passage to the limit

This subsection is devoted to the study of the tightness of the approximations solutions and the proof of several convergences which will enable us to pass to the limit and construct a weak probabilistic solution to our problem via the martingale representation theorem given in
[
12
, Theorem 8.2]
. For this purpose, we consider the following spaces:

(4.58)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{Z}_{n}=L_{w}^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))\cap L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];H^{-3}(\mathcal{O}))\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];L^{2}_{w}(\mathcal{O})),\\
&\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{u}}=L_{w}^{2}(0,T;V)\cap L^{2}(0,T;H)\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];V^{*})\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];H_{w}),\\
&\mathcal{Z}_{c}=L_{w}^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\mathcal{O}))\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];H_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{O})),\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{Z}_{n}\times\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{u}}\times\mathcal{Z}_{c}.\end{split}

By making appropriate use of Lemma
A.3
, Corollary
A.8
, and Corollary
A.9
, we will now show that the sequence of probability law
\mathcal{L}_{m}=\mathcal{L}(n_{m})\times\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{u}_{m})\times\mathcal{L}(c_{m})
is tight in
\mathcal{Z}
.

Lemma 4.11
.

We suppose that the hypotheses of Proposition
4.3
hold. Then the family of probability laws
(\mathcal{L}_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight on the space
\mathcal{Z}
.

Proof.

We firstly prove that
(\mathcal{L}(n_{m}))_{m}
is tight on
\mathcal{Z}_{n}
. For any
\varepsilon>0
we set
\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}=\eta_{0}e^{\mathcal{K}/\varepsilon}>\eta_{0}
where
\eta_{0}>1
is given by Lemma
4.5
. From the inequality (
4.51
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\sup_{m}\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\ \left|n_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2})}>\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{m}\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\ \eta_{0}\exp\left(\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}^{4}ds\right)>\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{m}\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\ \mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}^{4}ds>\ln\left(\frac{\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}}{\eta_{0}}\right)\right\}.

Using the Markov inequality and inequality (
4.55
), we infer that

\displaystyle\sup_{m}\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\ \left|n_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2})}>\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\ln\left(\frac{\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}}{\eta_{0}}\right)}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}^{4}ds\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\mathcal{K}}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}^{4}ds\right)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon.

Similarly, we can also prove that

\displaystyle\sup_{m}\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\ \left|n_{m}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1})}>\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{m}\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\ \eta_{0}\exp\left(\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}^{4}ds\right)>\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon.

Thanks to inequality (
4.56
) we derive that

\displaystyle\sup_{m}\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\ \left|n_{m}\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{C}^{1/2}([0,T];H^{-3})}>\frac{\mathcal{K}}{\varepsilon}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\mathcal{K}}\mathbb{E}\left|n_{m}\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{C}^{1/2}([0,T];H^{-3})}\leq\varepsilon.

Since these three last inequalities hold, we can apply Lemma
A.3
and conclude that the law of
n_{m}
form a family of probability measures which is tight on
\mathcal{Z}_{n}
.

Secondly, we will prove that the laws of
\mathbf{u}_{m}
and
c_{m}
are tight on
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{u}}\times\mathcal{Z}_{c}
. From inequalities (
4.52
) and (
4.54
), we obtain the first two conditions of Corollaries
A.8
and
A.9
for
\mathbf{u}_{m}
and
c_{m}
respectively. Hence, it is sufficient to prove that the sequences
(\mathbf{u}_{m})_{m}
and
(c_{m})
satisfy the Aldous condition in the spaces
V^{*}
and
L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
respectively. Let
\theta>0

(\tau_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
be a sequence of stopping times such that
0\leq\tau_{\ell}\leq T
. From the second equation of system (
4.2
) we have

\displaystyle c_{m}(\tau_{\ell}+\theta)-c_{m}(\tau_{\ell})

\displaystyle=\xi\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}A_{1}c_{m}(s)ds-\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))ds

(4.59)

\displaystyle+\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{1}(n_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))ds+\gamma\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(c_{m}(s))d\beta_{s}.

By the Fubini theorem, the Hölder inequality and inequality (
4.57
), we have the following estimates

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\xi\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}A_{1}c_{m}(s)ds\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\xi^{2}\theta^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\left|A_{1}c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\xi^{2}\theta^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|A_{1}c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\leq\mathcal{K}\theta^{1/2},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))ds\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\theta^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\theta^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\leq\mathcal{K}\theta^{1/2},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{1}(n_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))ds\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\theta^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{1}(n_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\theta^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{1}(n_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\leq\mathcal{K}\theta^{1/2}.

By the Itô isometry, we note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\gamma\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(c_{m}(s))d\beta_{s}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{2}\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\left|\phi(c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2},L^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\left|\sigma_{k}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\theta\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\mathcal{K}\theta.

Combining these inequalities, we infer from equality (
4.59
) that the condition (
A.5
) is satisfies for
(c_{m})_{m\geq 1}
in
L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
. Hence by Lemma
A.7
the sequence
(c_{m})_{m\geq 1}
satisfies the Aldous condition in the space
L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
.

Now we will consider the sequence
(\mathbf{u}_{m})_{m\geq 1}
. We first observe that from the first equation of system (
4.2
) we infer that

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{m}(\tau_{\ell}+\theta)-\mathbf{u}_{m}(\tau_{\ell})

\displaystyle=-\eta\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}A_{0}\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)ds-\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}B_{0}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))ds

(4.60)

\displaystyle+\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}R_{0}(n_{m}(s),\varPhi)ds+\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))dW_{s}.

Thanks to the Hölder inequality and (
4.57
), we have the following estimates

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\eta\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}A_{0}\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)ds\right|_{V^{*}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\eta^{2}\theta^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\left|A_{0}\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{V^{*}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\eta^{2}\theta^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|A_{0}\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{V^{*}}ds\leq\mathcal{K}\theta^{1/2},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{0}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))ds\right|_{V^{*}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\theta^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{V^{*}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\theta^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{V^{*}}ds\leq\mathcal{K}\theta^{1/2},

as well as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{0}(n_{m}(s),\varPhi)ds\right|_{V^{*}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\theta^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{0}(n_{m}(s),\varPhi)\right|^{2}_{V^{*}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\theta^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{0}(n_{m}(s),\varPhi)\right|^{2}_{V^{*}}ds\leq\mathcal{K}\theta^{1/2}.

Thanks to the Itô isometry and the assumption on
g
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))dW_{s}\right|^{2}_{V^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))dW_{s}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U},H)}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{\ell}}^{\tau_{\ell}+\theta}(1+\left|(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2})ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left(1+\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\right)\theta\leq\mathcal{K}\theta.

From these inequalities and equality (
4.60
), we can conclude by Lemma
A.7
that the sequence
(\mathbf{u}_{m})_{m\geq 1}
satisfies the Aldous condition in the space
V^{*}
.
Hence, by applying Corollary
A.8
and Corollary
A.9
, we see that the laws of
c_{m}
and
\mathbf{u}_{m}
are tight on
\mathcal{Z}_{c}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{u}}
, respectively.
∎

Since
(\mathcal{L}_{m})_{m}
is tight on
\mathcal{Z}
, invoking
[
28
, Corollary 2, Appendix B]
(see also
[
7
, Theorem 4.13]
) there exists a probability space

(\Omega^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime}),

and a subsequence of random
vectors
(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m_{k}},\bar{c}_{m_{k}},\bar{n}_{m_{k}})
with values in
\mathcal{Z}
such that

i):

(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m_{k}},\bar{c}_{m_{k}},\bar{n}_{m_{k}})
have the same probability distributions as
(\mathbf{u}_{m_{k}},c_{m_{k}},n_{m_{k}})
,

ii):

(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m_{k}},\bar{c}_{m_{k}},\bar{n}_{m_{k}})
converges in the topology of
\mathcal{Z}
to a random element
(\mathbf{u},c,n)\in\mathcal{Z}
with probability
1
on
(\Omega^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime})
as
k\to\infty
.

To simplify the notation, we will simply denote these sequences by
(\mathbf{u}_{m},c_{m},n_{m})_{m\geq 1}
and
(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\bar{c}_{m},\bar{n}_{m})_{m\geq 1}
, respectively.

Next, from the definition of the space
\mathcal{Z}
, we deduce that
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s.,

(4.61)

\begin{split}&\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\to\mathbf{u}\text{ in }L_{w}^{2}(0,T;V)\cap L^{2}(0,T;H)\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];V^{*})\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];H_{w}),\\
&\bar{c}_{m}\to c\text{ in }L_{w}^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\mathcal{O}))\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];H_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{O})),\\
