&\bar{n}_{m}\to n\text{ in }L_{w}^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))\cap L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];H^{-3}(\mathcal{O}))\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];L^{2}_{w}(\mathcal{O})).\end{split}

According to
[
40
, Theorem 1.10.4 and Addendum 1.10.5]
, a family of measurable map
\Psi_{m}:\Omega^{\prime}\to\Omega
can be constructed such that together with the new probability space
(\Omega^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime})
satisfy the property

(4.62)

\begin{split}&\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(\omega^{\prime})=\mathbf{u}_{m}\circ\Psi_{m}(\omega^{\prime}),\ \bar{n}_{m}(\omega^{\prime})=n_{m}\circ\Psi_{m}(\omega^{\prime}),\\
&\bar{c}_{m}(\omega^{\prime})=c_{m}\circ\Psi_{m}(\omega^{\prime}),\text{ and }\mathbb{P}=\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\circ\Psi^{-1}_{m},\end{split}

for all
\omega^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}
.
Taking into account the fact that inequality (
4.10
) holds, we can derive that for almost every
(t,\omega^{\prime})\in[0,T]\times\Omega^{\prime}
,

(4.63)

\left|\bar{c}_{m}(t,\omega^{\prime})\right|_{L^{\infty}}=\left|c_{m}(t,\Psi_{m}(\omega^{\prime}))\right|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}},\qquad\text{for all }m\geq 1.

Since the laws of
(\mathbf{u}_{m},c_{m},n_{m})
and
(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\bar{c}_{m},\bar{n}_{m})
are equal in the space
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{u}}\times\mathcal{Z}_{c}\times\mathcal{Z}_{n}
, we have the estimates (
4.52
), (
4.54
) and

(4.64)

\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{c}_{m}(s)\right|_{H^{2}}^{2}ds\leq\mathcal{K},\quad\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\leq\mathcal{K},\\

as well as

(4.65)

\qquad\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{n}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\leq\mathcal{K}.

From (
4.64
) and (
4.65
) and the Banach-Alaoglu Theorem, we conclude that, there exists a subsequence of
(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m})_{m\geq 1}
,
(\bar{c}_{m})_{m\geq 1}
, and
(\bar{n}_{m})_{m\geq 1}
weakly convergent in

L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime};L^{2}(0,T;V))
,
L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime};L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\mathcal{O})))
, and
L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime};L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O})))
respectively.
i.e.

(4.66)

\begin{split}&\mathbf{u}\in L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime};L^{2}(0,T;V)),\quad c\in L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime};L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\mathcal{O}))),\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad n\in L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime};L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))).\end{split}

On the other hand, from estimates (
4.52
), (
4.53
) and (
4.54
) of Corollary
4.8
, and the equalities given by (
4.62
), we get for any
p\geq 1
,

(4.67)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)\right|^{2p}_{L^{2}}+\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathcal{K},

(4.68)

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{n}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathcal{K},

(4.69)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\bar{c}_{m}(s)\right|^{p}_{H^{1}}+\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{c}_{m}(s)\right|_{H^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{p}\leq\mathcal{K}.

Then, invoking the Fatou lemma, we infer that for
p\geq 2
, we have

(4.70)

\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\mathbf{u}(s)\right|^{p}_{L^{2}}<\infty,\qquad\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|c(s)\right|^{p}_{H^{1}}<\infty.

and

(4.71)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{p}<\infty,\ \mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{p}<\infty,\ \mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c(s)\right|_{H^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{p}<\infty.

Now, we prove three lemmata which show how convergence in
\mathcal{Z}
given by (
4.61
) will be used for the convergence of the deterministic terms appearing in the Galerkin approximation. We start by noting that since
n_{0}^{m}
,
c_{0}^{m}
and
\mathbf{u}_{0}^{m}
have been chosen such that (
4.1
) holds, we can derive that for all
\psi\in H^{3}(\mathcal{O})
and
(\psi,\mathbf{v})\in L^{2}(\mathcal{O})\times V
,

(4.72)

\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}(n^{m}_{0},\psi)=(n_{0},\psi),\ \lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}(c^{m}_{0},\psi)=(c_{0},\psi),\text{ and }\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}(\mathbf{u}^{m}_{0},\mathbf{v})=(\mathbf{u}_{0},\mathbf{v}).

Lemma 4.12
.

For any
r,t\in[0,T]
with
r\leq t
and
\psi\in H^{3}(\mathcal{O})
, the following convergences hold
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s.

(4.73)

\begin{split}&\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}(\bar{n}_{m}(t),\psi)=(n(t),\psi),\\
&\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}\int_{r}^{t}(A_{1}\bar{n}_{m}(s),\psi)ds=\int_{r}^{t}(A_{1}n(s),\psi)ds\\
&\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}\int_{r}^{t}(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{n}_{m}(s)),\psi)ds=\int_{r}^{t}(B_{1}(\mathbf{u}(s),n(s)),\psi)ds,\\
&\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}\int_{r}^{t}(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{2}(\bar{n}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s)),\psi)ds=\int_{r}^{t}(R_{2}(n(s),c(s)),\psi)ds.\end{split}

Proof.

Let
\psi\in H^{3}(\mathcal{O})
and
t\in[0,T]
be arbitrary but fixed. By the Hölder inequality we have

\displaystyle\left|(\bar{n}_{m}(t),\psi)-(n(t),\psi)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\bar{n}_{m}(t)-n(t)\right|_{H^{-3}}\left|\psi\right|_{H^{3}}

(4.74)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\bar{n}_{m}-n\right|_{\mathcal{C}([0,T];H^{-3})}\left|\psi\right|_{H^{3}},

which along with (
4.61
) implies the first convergence in (
4.73
).

Now, we also fix
r\in[0,T]
such that
r\leq t
. By an integration-by-parts and the Hölder inequality we note that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{r}^{t}(A_{1}\bar{n}_{m}(s),\psi)ds-\int_{r}^{t}(A_{1}n(s),\psi)ds\right|dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|(A_{1}\bar{n}_{m}(s)-A_{1}n(s),\psi)\right|ds

(4.75)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|(\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s),A_{1}\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq T\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|(\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s),A_{1}\psi)\right|.

From the convergence (
4.61
) we infer that
\bar{n}_{m}\to n
in
\mathcal{C}([0,T];L_{w}^{2}(\mathcal{O})
,
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s. This means that
\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|(\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s),\varphi)\right|
tends to zero for all
\varphi\in L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
as
m
goes to infinity with probability one. We plug
\varphi=A_{1}\psi
and pass to the limit in (
4.75
) and derive the second convergence of (
4.73
).
We have for all
\omega\in\Omega
,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{r}^{t}(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{n}_{m}(s)),\psi)ds-\int_{r}^{t}(B_{1}(\mathbf{u}(s),n(s)),\psi)ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|(B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{n}_{m}(s)),\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi)\right|+\int_{0}^{T}\left|(B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{n}_{m}(s))-B_{1}(\mathbf{u}(s),n(s)),\psi)\right|ds

Since
\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\to\mathbf{u}
in
L^{2}(0,T;H)
, and
\bar{n}_{m}\to n
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))

\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s., by integration-by-parts, we derive that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left|(B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{n}_{m}(s)),\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|(\bar{n}_{m}(s)\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\nabla(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi))\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\nabla(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi)\right|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{n}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi\right|_{H^{3}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{n}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi\right|_{H^{3}}.

By using an integration-by-parts and the fact that
\nabla\cdot\mathbf{u}=0
we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left|(B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{n}_{m}(s))-B_{1}(\mathbf{u}(s),n(s)),\psi),\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|((\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)-\mathbf{u}(s))\nabla\bar{n}_{m}(s),\psi)\right|ds+\int_{0}^{T}\left|(\mathbf{u}(s)\nabla(\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s)),\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|(\bar{n}_{m}(s),(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)-\mathbf{u}(s))\cdot\nabla\psi)\right|ds+\int_{0}^{T}\left|((\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s)),\mathbf{u}(s)\cdot\nabla\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq\left|\psi\right|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)-\mathbf{u}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\bar{n}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}ds+\left|\nabla\psi\right|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s)\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\mathbf{u}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}ds.

Using the fact that
\left|\nabla\psi\right|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\left|\psi\right|_{H^{3}}
, we infer from the two last inequalities that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{n}_{m}(s)),\psi)ds-\int_{0}^{t}(B_{1}(\mathbf{u}(s),n(s)),\psi)ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq T\left|\psi\right|_{H^{3}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)-\mathbf{u}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{n}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}

(4.76)

\displaystyle\qquad{}+T\left|\psi\right|_{H^{3}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}+\mathcal{K}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi\right|_{H^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)-\mathbf{u}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\mathcal{K}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}+\mathcal{K}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi\right|_{H^{3}},

which upon letting
n\to\infty
, implies the third convergence in (
4.73
).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{r}^{t}(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{2}(\bar{n}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s)),\psi)ds-\int_{r}^{t}(R_{2}(n(s),c(s)),\psi)ds\right|

(4.77)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|(R_{2}(\bar{n}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))-R_{2}(n(s),c(s)),\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|(R_{2}(\bar{n}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s)),\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi)\right|ds.

Since
(\bar{c}_{m},\bar{n}_{m})\to(c,n)
in
\mathcal{Z}_{c}\times\mathcal{Z}_{n}
, we see that
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left|(R_{2}(\bar{n}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s)),\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq\left|\nabla(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi)\right|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{n}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla\bar{c}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi\right|_{H^{3}}.

On the other hand, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left|(R_{2}(\bar{n}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))-R_{2}(n(s),c(s)),\psi),\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|((\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s))\nabla\bar{c}_{m}(s),\nabla\psi)\right|ds+\int_{0}^{T}\left|(n(s)\nabla(\bar{c}_{m}(s)-c(s)),\nabla\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq\left|\nabla\psi\right|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s)\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla\bar{c}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\left|\nabla\psi\right|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla(\bar{c}_{m}(s)-c(s))\right|_{L^{2}}\left|n(s)\right|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\mathcal{K}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla(\bar{c}_{m}(s)-c(s))\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2},

which along with (
4.61
) implies the fourth convergence in (
4.73
).
∎

Lemma 4.13
.

For any
r,t\in[0,T]
with
r\leq t
and
\psi\in H^{2}(\mathcal{O})
, the following convergences hold
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s.

(4.78)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}(\bar{c}_{m}(t),\psi)=(c(t),\psi),\\
&\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}\int_{r}^{t}(A_{1}\bar{c}_{m}(s),\psi)ds=\int_{r}^{t}(A_{1}c(s),\psi)ds,\\
&\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}\int_{r}^{t}(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s)),\psi)ds=\int_{r}^{t}(B_{1}(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s)),\psi)ds,\\
&\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}\int_{r}^{t}(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{1}(\bar{n}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s)),\psi)ds=\int_{r}^{t}(R_{1}(n(s),c(s)),\psi)ds.\end{split}

Proof.

Since
\bar{c}_{m}\to c
in
\mathcal{C}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))
,
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s., the first convergence is done exactly using a similarly inequality as (
4.74
).
By an integration by part and the Hölder inequality we note that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{r}^{t}(A_{1}\bar{c}_{m}(s),\psi)ds-\int_{r}^{t}(A_{1}c(s),\psi)ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|(A_{1}\bar{c}_{m}(s)-A_{1}c(s),\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|(\nabla(\bar{c}_{m}(s)-c(s)),\nabla\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq T^{1/2}\left|\psi\right|_{H^{1}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{c}_{m}(s)-c(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}}ds\right)^{1/2},

which altogether with (
4.61
) implies the second convergence in (
4.78
).

Next, using the Sobolev embedding
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow L^{4}(\mathcal{O})
, we get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{r}^{t}(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s)),\psi)ds-\int_{r}^{t}(B_{1}(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s)),\psi)ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|(B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))-B_{1}(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s)),\psi),\psi)\right|ds+\int_{0}^{T}\left|(B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s)),\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|((\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)-\mathbf{u}(s))\nabla\bar{c}_{m}(s),\psi)\right|ds+\int_{0}^{T}\left|(\mathbf{u}(s)\nabla(\bar{c}_{m}(s)-c(s)),\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+T^{1/2}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi\right|_{L^{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\psi\right|_{L^{4}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)-\mathbf{u}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla\bar{c}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}ds+\left|\psi\right|_{L^{4}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla(\bar{c}_{m}(s)-c(s))\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\mathbf{u}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\mathcal{K}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi\right|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq T\left|\psi\right|_{H^{1}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)-\mathbf{u}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\bar{c}_{m}(s)\right|_{H^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+T\left|\psi\right|_{H^{1}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla(\bar{c}_{m}(s)-c(s))\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}ds+\mathcal{K}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi\right|_{L^{2}}.

Since the convergence (
4.61
) holds, we arrive at

\displaystyle\left|\int_{r}^{t}(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s)),\psi)ds-\int_{r}^{t}(B_{1}(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s)),\psi)ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq T\left|\psi\right|_{H^{1}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)-\mathbf{u}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{c}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad{}+T\left|\psi\right|_{H^{1}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{c}_{m}(s)-c(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}}ds\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathcal{K}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi\right|_{L^{2}},

which along with (
4.61
) implies the third convergence in (
4.78
).

Now we prove the last convergence. To this purpose, we note that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{r}^{t}(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{1}(\bar{n}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s)),\psi)ds-\int_{r}^{t}(R_{1}(n(s),c(s)),\psi)ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|(R_{1}(\bar{n}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))-R_{1}(n(s),c(s)),\psi)\right|ds

(4.79)

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|(R_{1}(\bar{n}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s)),\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|((\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s))f(\bar{c}_{m}(s)),\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|n(s)(f(\bar{c}_{m}(s))-f(c(s))),\psi)\right|ds+\mathcal{K}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\psi-\psi\right|_{L^{2}}.

Using (
4.63
), we derive that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left|((\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s))f(\bar{c}_{m}(s)),\psi)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq\left|\psi\right|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left|\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s)\right|\left|f(\bar{c}_{m}(s))\right|dxds

\displaystyle\leq T^{1/2}\left|\mathcal{O}\right|^{1/2}\left|\psi\right|_{H^{2}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f(s)\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{n}_{m}(s)-n(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}.

In a similar way, we see that

(4.80)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\left|n(s,x)(f(\bar{c}_{m}(s,x))-f(c(s,x))),\psi)\right|dsdx\\
&\leq\left|\psi\right|_{H^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left|n(s,x)f(\bar{c}_{m}(s,x))-n(s,x)f(c(s,x))\right|dxds.\end{split}

Since the strong convergence
\bar{c}_{m}\to c
in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))
,
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s., holds, we derive that up to a subsequence

\bar{c}_{m}\to c\qquad dt\otimes dx\text{-a.e}

Owing to the fact that
f
is continuous, we infer that
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s.,

nf(\bar{c}_{m})\to nf(c)\qquad a.e\text{ in }\times(0,T)\times\mathcal{O}.

We also note that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,
\{nf(\bar{c}_{m})\}_{m\geq 1}
is uniformly integrable over
(0,T)\times\mathcal{O}
. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\int_{(0,T)\times\mathcal{O}}\left|n(s,x)f(\bar{c}_{m}(s,x))\right|^{2}dxdsd\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{0\leq s\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{2}(s)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left|n(s,x)\right|^{2}dxds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{T}\left|n(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds.

Therefore, by the Vitali Convergence Theorem, we derive that
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s., the right answer of the inequality (
4.80
) tends to zero as
m
tends to
\infty
. Owing to this result, we can pass to the limit in the inequality (
4.79
) and obtain the last convergence of (
4.78
).
∎

Next we prove the following convergences.

Lemma 4.14
.

For any
r,t\in[0,T]
with
r\leq t
and
\mathbf{v}\in V
, the following convergences hold
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s.

(4.81)

\begin{split}&\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(t),\mathbf{v})=(\mathbf{u}(t),\mathbf{v}),\\
&\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}\int_{r}^{t}(A_{0}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\mathbf{v})ds=\int_{r}^{t}(A_{0}\mathbf{u}(s),\mathbf{v})ds,\\
&\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}\int_{r}^{t}(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}B_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)),\mathbf{v})ds=\int_{r}^{t}(B_{0}(\mathbf{u}(s),\mathbf{u}(s)),\mathbf{v})ds,\\
&\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}\int_{r}^{t}(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}R_{0}(\bar{n}_{m}(s),\varPhi),\mathbf{v})ds=\int_{r}^{t}(R_{0}(n(s),\varPhi),\mathbf{v})ds.\end{split}

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of Lemma
4.12
and Lemma
4.13
.
∎

In what follows, we will combine the convergence result from Lemma
4.12
, Lemma
4.13
and Lemma
4.14
as well as martingale representation theorem to construct a probabilistic weak solution to the problem (
1.2
). In order to simplify the notation, we define on the probability space
(\Omega^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime})
the processes
N_{m}^{1}
,
N_{m}^{2}
, and
N_{m}^{3}
, respectively, by for
t\in[0,T]
,

N_{m}^{1}(t):=-\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(t)-\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}[\eta A_{0}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s)+\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}B_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s))]ds+\mathbf{u}^{m}_{0}+\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}R_{0}(\bar{n}_{m}(s),\varPhi)ds,

N_{m}^{2}(t):=-\bar{c}_{m}(t)-\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}[\xi A_{1}\bar{c}_{m}(s)+\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))]ds+c^{m}_{0}-\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{1}(\bar{n}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))ds,

and

N_{m}^{3}(t):=-\bar{n}_{m}(t)-\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}[\delta A_{1}\bar{n}_{m}(s)+\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{n}_{m}(s))]ds+n^{m}_{0}-\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{2}(\bar{n}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))ds.

Lemma 4.15
.

For all
m\in\mathbb{N}
and for any
t\in[0,T]
, we have

(4.82)

N_{m}^{3}(t)=0,\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\text{-a.s.}

Proof.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
t\in[0,T]
be arbitrary but fixed. On the probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
, we define the processes
M_{m}^{3}(t)
by

M_{m}^{3}(t):=-n_{m}(t)-\int_{0}^{t}[\delta A_{1}n_{m}(s)+\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),n_{m}(s))]ds+n_{0}^{m}-\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{2}(n_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))ds.

We also define the following subsets of
\Omega
and
\Omega^{\prime}

\mathcal{A}_{m}^{N}(t):=\left\{\omega^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}:N_{m}^{3}(t)=0\right\}\text{ and }\mathcal{A}_{m}^{M}(t):=\left\{\omega\in\Omega:M_{m}^{3}(t)=0\right\}.

We note that, since the last equation of (
4.2
) holds,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{M}(t))=1
. Furthermore, by (
4.62
), we derive that for all
\omega^{\prime}\in\Omega
,
N_{m}^{3}(t,\omega^{\prime})=M_{m}^{3}(t,\Psi_{m}(\omega^{\prime}))
and therefore we observe that
\mathcal{A}_{m}^{N}(t)=\Psi_{m}^{-1}(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{M}(t))
. Invoking (
4.62
) once more, we deduce that

\mathbb{P}^{\prime}(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{N}(t))=\mathbb{P}^{\prime}(\Psi^{-1}_{m}(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{M}(t)))=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{M}(t))=1,

which completes the proof of Lemma
4.15
.
∎

Using the convergences (
4.72
) and (
4.73
) as well as
Lemma
4.15
we see that for all
t\in[0,T]
,
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s.

(4.83)

n(t)+\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}[\delta A_{1}n(s)+B_{1}(\mathbf{u}(s),n(s))]ds=n_{0}-\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}R_{2}(n(s),c(s))ds,\qquad\text{in }H^{-3}(\mathcal{O}).

Now, on the probability space
(\Omega^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime})
we define a the
\mathbf{H}_{m}\times H_{m}
-valued processes
N_{m}
by
N_{m}(t)=(N^{1}_{m}(t),N^{2}_{m}(t))
for all
m\geq 1
and
t\in[0,T]
. Since

(4.84)

\mathbf{H}_{m}\times H_{m}\subset H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow V^{*}\times H^{-2}(\mathcal{O}),

the process
N_{m}
can be seen as a
V^{*}\times H^{-2}(\mathcal{O})
-valued process.

Next, we collect the necessary ingredients for the application of the martingale representation theorem from
[
12
, Theorem 8.2]
. To this aim, we consider the following Gelfand triple
V\hookrightarrow H\hookrightarrow V^{*}
and
H^{2}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow H^{-2}(\mathcal{O})
. Let
i^{1}:V\hookrightarrow H
be the usual embedding and
i^{1*}
its Hilbert-space-adjoint such that
(ix,y)=(x,i^{1*}y)_{V}
for all
x\in V
and
y\in H
. In a very similar way, we denote the usual embedding
H^{2}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
by
i^{2}
and by
i^{*2}
its Hilbert-space-adjoint.
We define the embedding
i:V\times H^{2}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
and its adjoint
i^{*}:H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})\longrightarrow V\times H^{2}(\mathcal{O})
respectively by

i=\begin{pmatrix}i^{1}&0\\
0&i^{2}\end{pmatrix},\qquad i^{*}=\begin{pmatrix}i^{1*}&0\\
0&i^{2*}\end{pmatrix}.

Further, we set
L^{1}=(i^{1*})^{\prime}:V^{*}\longrightarrow H
as the dual operator of
i^{1*}
such that for all
x\in H
and
y\in V^{*}
,
(Ly,x)=\langle y,x\rangle
. Similarly, the dual operator of
i^{2*}
will be denoted by
L^{2}:H^{-2}(\mathcal{O})\longrightarrow L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
. We then define the following dual operator
L:=(i^{*})^{\prime}:V^{*}\times H^{-2}(\mathcal{O})\longrightarrow H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
by

L=\begin{pmatrix}L^{1}&0\\
0&L^{2}\end{pmatrix}.

On the space
\mathbf{H}_{m}\times H_{m}
, we define a mapping
G_{m}
by

G_{m}(\mathbf{v},\psi)=\begin{pmatrix}L^{1}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\mathbf{v},\psi)&0\\
0&L^{2}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(\psi)\end{pmatrix},\qquad(\mathbf{v},\psi)\in\mathbf{H}_{m}\times H_{m}.

Here
(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\mathbf{v},\psi),\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(\psi))=(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\mathbf{v},\psi),\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(\psi))
is seen as an element of
V^{*}\times H^{-2}(\mathcal{O})
owing to the inclusion (
4.84
).

In the following lemma, we prove the martingale property of the process
LN_{m}
.

Lemma 4.16
.

For each
m\geq 1
, the process
LN_{m}
is an
H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
-valued continuous square integrable martingale with respect to the filtration

\mathbb{F}^{{}^{\prime}m}=\left\{\sigma\left(\sigma\left((\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s),\bar{n}_{m}(s));s\leq t\right)\cup\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\right)\right\}_{t\in[0,T]},

where
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
is the set of null sets of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
. The quadratic variation of
LN_{m}
is given by

(4.85)

\langle\langle LN_{m}\rangle\rangle_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))^{*}ds,

where
G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\bar{c}_{m})^{*}:H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})\to\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
is the adjoint of the operator
G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\bar{c}_{m})
and is given by

G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\bar{c}_{m})^{*}\mathbf{v}=\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\bar{c}_{m})e_{k},i^{1*}\mathbf{w})e_{k},\sum_{k=1}^{2}(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(\bar{c}_{m})g_{k},i^{2*}\psi)g_{k}\right),

for all
\mathbf{v}=(\mathbf{w},\psi)\in H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
.

Proof.

For any
m\geq 1
we define the
V^{*}\times H^{-2}(\mathcal{O})
-valued processes
M_{m}
by

M_{m}(t)=(M^{1}_{m}(t),M^{2}_{m}(t)),\quad t\in[0,T],

where

M_{m}^{1}(t):=-\mathbf{u}_{m}(t)-\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}[\eta A_{0}\mathbf{u}_{m}(s)+\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}B_{0}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),\mathbf{u}_{m}(s))]ds+\mathbf{u}^{m}_{0}+\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}R_{0}(n_{m}(s),\varPhi)ds,

M_{m}^{2}(t):=-c_{m}(t)-\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}[\xi A_{1}c_{m}(s)+\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}B_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))]ds+c^{m}_{0}-\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}R_{1}(n_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))ds.

Let us set
\mathbf{W}_{s}:=(W_{s},\beta_{s})
. Then, since
(\mathbf{u}_{m},c_{m},n_{m})
is a solution of the finite dimensional system (
4.2
), we deduce that
LM_{m}
can be represented as

LM_{m}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}G_{m}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))d\mathbf{W}_{s},\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}\quad\text{for all }t\in[0.T].

Using the continuity property of the operators
L^{1}
and
L^{2}
as well as Corollary
4.8
, the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|G_{m}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2},H\times L^{2})}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U},H)}ds+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(c_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2},L^{2})}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}(1+\left|(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2})ds+\gamma^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\left|\sigma_{k}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left(1+\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))\right|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\right)+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\nabla c_{m}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}<\infty,

yields that
M_{m}
is a square integrable continuous martingale over the probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]},\mathbb{P})
. Moreover, from the definition of
M_{m}
we derive that for each
t\in[0.T]
,
M_{m}(t)
is measurable with respect to the
\sigma
-field

\mathbb{F}^{m}=\left\{\sigma\left(\sigma\left((\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s),n_{m}(s));s\leq t\right)\cup\mathcal{N}\right)\right\}_{t\in[0,T]},

where
\mathcal{N}
is the set of null sets of
\mathcal{F}
. Hence, invoking
[
12
, Theorem 4.27]
we infer that
M_{m}
is a
\mathbb{F}^{m}
-martingale with quadratic variation

\langle\langle M_{m}\rangle\rangle_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}G_{m}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))G_{m}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))^{*}ds.

This means that for all
s,t\in[0,T]
,
s\leq t
, all
\mathbf{v}_{i}=(\mathbf{w}_{i},\psi_{i})\in H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
,
i=1,2
, and all bounded and continuous real-valued functions
h=(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})
on
\mathcal{C}([0,T];H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(LM_{m}(t)-LM_{m}(s),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}h_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{2}(c_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{3}(n_{m}|_{[0,s]})\right]=0,

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left(LM_{m}(t),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\left(LM_{m}(t),\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}-\left(LM_{m}(s),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\left(LM_{m}(s),\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\left.-\int_{0}^{t}\left(G_{m}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1},G_{m}(\mathbf{u}_{m}(s),c_{m}(s))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}ds\right)\times\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\times h_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{2}(c_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{3}(n_{m}|_{[0,s]})\right]=0.

Since
(\mathbf{u}_{m},c_{m},n_{m})
and
(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\bar{c}_{m},\bar{n}_{m})
have the same laws on
\mathcal{C}([0,T];\mathcal{H}_{m})
, we deduce from these two last equalities that

(4.86)

\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left[\left(LN_{m}(t)-LN_{m}(s),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}h_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{2}(\bar{c}_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{3}(\bar{n}_{m}|_{[0,s]})\right]=0,

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left[\left(\left(LN_{m}(t),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\left(LN_{m}(t),\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad-\left(LN_{m}(s),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\left(LN_{m}(s),\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\right.\right.

(4.87)

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad-\int_{0}^{t}\left(G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1},G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}ds\right)\times\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\times h_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{2}(\bar{c}_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{3}(\bar{n}_{m}|_{[0,s]})\right]=0,

for all
s,t\in[0,T]
,
s\leq t
, all
\mathbf{v}_{i}=(\mathbf{w}_{i},\psi_{i})\in H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
,
i=1,2
, and all (real-valued) function
h_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
bounded and continuous on
\mathcal{C}([0,T];\mathcal{H}_{m})
,
\mathcal{C}([0,T];H_{m})
, and
\mathcal{C}([0,T];H_{m})
respectively,
This implies that
LN_{m}
is a continuous square integrable martingale with respect to
\mathbb{F}^{{}^{\prime}m}
and the quadratic variation is given as claimed by equality (
4.85
).
∎

On the new probability space
(\Omega^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime})
, we consider the
V^{*}\times H^{-2}(\mathcal{O})
-valued continuous process
N
defined by
N(t)=(N^{1}(t),N^{2}(t))
for all
t\in[0,T]
, where

N^{1}(t):=-\mathbf{u}(t)-\int_{0}^{t}[\eta A_{0}\mathbf{u}(s)+B_{0}(\mathbf{u}(s),\mathbf{u}(s))]ds+\mathbf{u}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}R_{0}(n(s),\varPhi)ds,

N^{2}(t):=-c(t)-\int_{0}^{t}[\xi A_{1}c(s)+B_{1}(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))]ds+c_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}R_{1}(n(s),c(s))ds.

In the next lemma, we state that
LN=(L^{1}N^{1},L^{2}N^{2})
is also an
H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
-valued martingale.

Lemma 4.17
.

The process
LN
is an
H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
-valued continuous square integrable martingale with respect to the filtration
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}=\left\{\sigma\left((\mathbf{u}(s),c(s),n(s));s\leq t\right)\right\}_{t\in[0,T]}
. The quadratic variation is given by

\langle\langle LN\rangle\rangle_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}G(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))G(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))^{*}ds,

where

G(\mathbf{u},c)=\begin{pmatrix}L^{1}g(\mathbf{u},c)&0\\
0&L^{2}\phi(c)\end{pmatrix},

and
G(\mathbf{u},c)^{*}:H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})\to\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
is the adjoint of the operator
G(\mathbf{u},c)
given by

G(\mathbf{u},c)^{*}\mathbf{v}=\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(L^{1}g(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))e_{k},\mathbf{w})e_{k},\sum_{k=1}^{2}(L^{2}\phi(c(s))g_{k},\psi)g_{k}\right),

for all
\mathbf{v}=(\mathbf{w},\psi)\in H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
.

Proof.

Let
t\in[0,T]
. We first prove that
LN
is an
H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
-valued square integrable random variable. Thanks to the continuity of
L
, it will be sufficient to prove that
\mathbb{E}\left|N\right|_{V^{*}\times H^{-2}}^{2}<\infty
. Using Lemma
4.13
and Lemma
4.14
, we conclude that

\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}N_{m}(t)=N(t)\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\text{-a.s. in }\ V^{*}\times H^{-2}(\mathcal{O}).

By the continuity of the injection
H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow V^{*}\times H^{-2}(\mathcal{O})
, the Burkholder-Gundy-Davis inequality for continuous martingales and equality (
4.85
) as well as inequalities (
4.67
) and (
4.69
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|N_{m}(s)\right|_{V^{*}\times H^{-2}}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|N_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}\times L^{2}}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2},H\times L^{2})}ds\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=2\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U},H)}ds\right)^{2}

(4.88)

\displaystyle\qquad{}+2\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(\bar{c}_{m}(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2},L^{2})}ds\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left(1+\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(s),\bar{c}_{m}(s))\right|_{\mathcal{H}}^{4}\right)+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\nabla\bar{c}_{m}(s)\right|^{4}_{L^{2}}<\mathcal{K}.

Hence, by the Vitali Theorem, we infer that
N(t)\in L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime};V^{*}\times H^{-2}(\mathcal{O}))
and

\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}N_{m}(t)=N(t)\ \text{ in }\ L^{2}(\Omega^{\prime};V^{*}\times H^{-2}(\mathcal{O})).

Next, let
\mathbf{v}=(\mathbf{w},\psi)\in V^{*}\times H^{-2}(\mathcal{O})
, and
h_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
be a bounded and continuous function on
\mathcal{C}([0,T];V^{*})
,
\mathcal{C}([0,T];H^{-2}(\mathcal{O}))
, and
\mathcal{C}([0,T];H^{-3}(\mathcal{O}))
respectively. Let
s,t\in[0,T]
such that
s\leq t
. Let

\displaystyle F_{m}(t,s):=\left(LN_{m}(t)-LN_{m}(s),\mathbf{v}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}h_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{2}(\bar{c}_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{3}(\bar{n}_{m}|_{[0,s]}),

\displaystyle F(t,s):=\left(LN(t)-LN(s),\mathbf{v}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}h_{1}(\mathbf{u}|_{[0,s]})h_{2}(c|_{[0,s]})h_{3}(n|_{[0,s]}).

We will prove that

(4.89)

\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}F_{m}(t,s)=\mathbb{E}^{\prime}F(t,s).

To this aim, we start by noting that by the
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s.-convergence
(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\bar{c}_{m},\bar{n}_{m})\to(\mathbf{u},c,n)
in
\mathcal{Z}
and Lemma
4.13
as well as Lemma
4.14
, we infer that

\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}F_{m}(t,s)=F(t,s),\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\text{-a.s. }

We will now show that the function
\{F_{m}(t,s)\}_{m\geq 1}
are uniformly integrable.
We use the estimate (
4.88
) to derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left|F_{m}(t,s)\right|^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|h_{1}\right|^{4}_{L^{\infty}}\left|h_{2}\right|^{4}_{L^{\infty}}\left|h_{3}\right|^{4}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\mathbf{v}\right|^{4}_{H\times L^{2}}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left[\left|N_{m}(t)\right|_{L^{2}\times L^{2}}^{4}+\left|N_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}\times L^{2}}^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|h_{1}\right|^{4}_{L^{\infty}}\left|h_{2}\right|^{4}_{L^{\infty}}\left|h_{3}\right|^{4}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\mathbf{v}\right|^{4}_{H\times L^{2}}.

Invoking the Vitali Theorem, we get the convergence (
4.89
).

Let
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
and
\mathbf{v}_{i}=(\mathbf{w}_{i},\psi_{i})\in H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
,
i=1,2
. Let

\displaystyle Q_{m}(t,s):

\displaystyle=\left(\left(LN_{m}(t),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\left(LN_{m}(t),\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\left(LN_{m}(s),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\left(LN_{m}(s),\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\right)h_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{2}(\bar{c}_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{3}(\bar{n}_{m}|_{[0,s]}),

\displaystyle Q(t,s):

\displaystyle=\left(\left(LN(t),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\left(LN(t),\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\left(LN(s),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\left(LN(s),\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\right)h_{1}(\mathbf{u}|_{[0,s]})h_{2}(c|_{[0,s]})h_{3}(n|_{[0,s]}).

Our purpose now is to prove that

(4.90)

\mathbb{E}^{\prime}Q(t,s)=\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}Q_{m}(t,s),

imitating the proof before. Indeed, by
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s.-convergence
(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\bar{c}_{m},\bar{n}_{m})\to(\mathbf{u},c,n)
in
\mathcal{Z}
and Lemma
4.13
as well as Lemma
4.14
once more, we obtain

\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}Q_{m}(t,s)=Q(t,s),\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\text{-a.s. }

We now prove the uniform integrability of
Q_{m}(t,s)
. For this purpose, by (
4.88
) we find that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left|Q_{m}(t,s)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|h_{1}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|h_{2}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|h_{3}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left[\left|\left(N_{m}(t),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\left(N_{m}(t),\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\right|^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad{}\left.+\left|\left(N_{m}(s),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\left(N_{m}(s),\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|h_{1}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|h_{2}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|h_{3}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\mathbf{v}_{1}\right|^{2}_{H\times L^{2}}\left|\mathbf{v}_{2}\right|^{2}_{H\times L^{2}}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left[\left|N_{m}(t)\right|_{L^{2}\times L^{2}}^{4}+\left|N_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}\times L^{2}}^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|h_{1}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|h_{2}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|h_{3}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\mathbf{v}\right|^{2}_{H\times L^{2}}.

As before, the Vitali Theorem yields equality (
4.90
).

Finally, we also define

\displaystyle R_{m}(t,s):=

\displaystyle\left(\int_{s}^{t}\left(G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1},G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}dr\right)\times

\displaystyle\times h_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{2}(\bar{c}_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{3}(\bar{n}_{m}|_{[0,s]}),

and

R(t,s):=\left(\int_{s}^{t}\left(G(\mathbf{u}(r),c(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1},G(\mathbf{u}(r),c(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}dr\right)h_{1}(\mathbf{u}|_{[0,s]})h_{2}(c|_{[0,s]})h_{3}(n|_{[0,s]}),

We claim that

(4.91)

\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}R_{m}(t,s)=\mathbb{E}^{\prime}R(t,s).

In order to establish this claim we first show that

(4.92)

\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}R_{m}(t,s)=R(t,s),\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\text{-a.s}.

Since
h_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{2}(\bar{c}_{m}|_{[0,s]})h_{3}(\bar{n}_{m}|_{[0,s]})\to h_{1}(\mathbf{u}|_{[0,s]})h_{2}(c|_{[0,s]})h_{3}(n|_{[0,s]})

\mathbb{P}
-a.s., in order to prove (
4.92
), it is sufficient to prove that

\displaystyle\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}\int_{s}^{t}\left(G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1},G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}dr

(4.93)

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\left(G(\mathbf{u}(r),c(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1},G(\mathbf{u}(r),c(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}dr,\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\text{-a.s}.

For all
r\in[s,t]
, we set

\displaystyle J(r):=

\displaystyle\left(G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1},G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}-\left(LG(\mathbf{u}(r),c(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1},LG(\mathbf{u}(r),c(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}.

Then, we note that

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\left|J(r)\right|dz

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|\left(G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1}-LG(\mathbf{u}(r),c(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1},G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\right|dr

(4.94)

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|\left(G(\mathbf{u}(r),c(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1},G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}-G(\mathbf{u}(r),c(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\right|dr

\displaystyle=I_{1}(m)+I_{2}(m).

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the Hölder inequality, we derive that

\displaystyle I_{1}(m)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1}-G(\mathbf{u}(r),c(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1})\right|_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right|_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Owing to the fact that
\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\bar{c}_{m})e_{k}\in H
and
\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(\bar{c}_{m})g_{k}\in L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
, we infer that

(L^{1}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\bar{c}_{m})e_{k},\mathbf{w}_{1}):=\langle\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\bar{c}_{m})e_{k},i^{1*}\mathbf{w}_{1}\rangle=(g(\mathbf{u},c)e_{k},i^{1*}\mathbf{w}_{1}).

and

(L^{2}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(\bar{c}_{m})g_{k},\psi_{2}):=\langle\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(\bar{c}_{m})g_{k},i^{2*}\psi_{2}\rangle=(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(\bar{c}_{m})g_{k},i^{2*}\psi_{2}).

Thus, using the inequality (
2.12
) and the fact that
\{e_{k}\}_{k\geq 1}
and
\{g_{k}\}_{k=1,2}
are orthonormal basis of
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathbb{R}^{2}
respectively, we derive that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left|G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right|_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}dr

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(L^{1}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))e_{k},\mathbf{w}_{2})e_{k}\right|_{\mathcal{U}}^{2}+\left|\sum_{k=1}^{2}(L^{2}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(\bar{c}_{m}(r))g_{k},\psi_{2})g_{k}\right|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}\right)dr

(4.95)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left|(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))e_{k},i^{1*}\mathbf{w}_{2})\right|^{2}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\left|(\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(\bar{c}_{m}(r))g_{k},i^{2*}\psi_{2})\right|^{2}dr

\displaystyle\leq\left|i^{1*}\mathbf{w}_{2}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|g(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U},H)}dr+\left|i^{2*}\psi_{2}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\phi(\bar{c}_{m}(r))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2},L^{2})}dr

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{T}(1+\left|(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}})dr+\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla\bar{c}_{m}(r))\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}dr

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K},\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\text{-a.s.}

In the last line we used the fact that
\bar{c}_{m}\to c
in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))
and
\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\to\mathbf{u}
in
L^{2}(0,T;H)

\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s.

On the other hand, we note that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left|G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1}-G(\mathbf{u}(r),c(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1})\right|_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}dr

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(L^{1}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}g(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))e_{k},\mathbf{w}_{1})-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(L^{1}g(\mathbf{u}(r),c(r))e_{k},\mathbf{w}_{1})\right]e_{k}\right|_{\mathcal{U}}^{2}dr

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|\left[\sum_{k=1}^{2}(L^{2}\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}\phi(\bar{c}_{m}(r))g_{k},\psi_{1})-\sum_{k=1}^{2}(L^{2}\phi(c(r))g_{k},\psi_{1})\right]g_{k}\right|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}dr.

Then by this last inequality and the inequality (
4.95
), we infer that

\displaystyle I_{1}^{2}(m)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(g(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))e_{k},\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}i^{1*}\mathbf{w}_{1})-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(g(\mathbf{u}(r),c(r))e_{k},i^{1*}\mathbf{w}_{1})\right|^{2}dr

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{2}(\phi(\bar{c}_{m}(r))g_{k},\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}i^{2*}\psi_{1})-\sum_{k=1}^{2}(\phi(c(r))g_{k},i^{2*}\psi_{1})\right|^{2}dr

(4.96)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left|i^{1*}\mathbf{w}_{1}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|g(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))-g(\mathbf{u}(r),c(r))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U},H)}dr

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\mathcal{K}\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{1}i^{1*}\mathbf{w}_{1}-i^{1*}\mathbf{w}_{1}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|g(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U},H)}dr

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\left|i^{2*}\psi_{1}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\phi(\bar{c}_{m}(r))-\phi(c(r))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2},L^{2})}dr

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\left|\mathcal{P}_{m}^{2}i^{2*}\psi_{1}-i^{2*}\psi_{1}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\phi(\bar{c}_{m}(r))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2},L^{2})}dr

\displaystyle:=II_{1}(m)+II_{2}(m)+II_{3}(m)+II_{4}(m).

By means of the continuity of
g
, the
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s.-convergence
(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\bar{c}_{m},\bar{n}_{m})\to(\mathbf{u},c,n)
in
\mathcal{Z}
, the inequality (
2.12
) and the Vitali Theorem, we can derive that
\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}II_{1}(m)=0
. Furthermore, since

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left|g(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U},H)}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\left|\phi(\bar{c}_{m}(r))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2},L^{2})}dr

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{T}(1+\left|(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}})dr+\mathcal{K}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla\bar{c}_{m}(r)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}dr

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\text{-a.s.},

we deduce that

\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}II_{2}(m)=\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}II_{4}(m)=0.

Now, we study the
II_{3}(m)
. We see that

\displaystyle II_{3}(m)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\psi_{1}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\gamma^{2}\left|\sigma\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla\bar{c}_{m}(r)-\nabla c(r)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}dr

\displaystyle\leq\left|\psi_{1}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\gamma^{2}\left|\sigma\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\bar{c}_{m}(r)-c(r)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}}dr.

By using the fact that
\bar{c}_{m}\to c
in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))
,
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-a.s., we can pass to the limit in this last inequality and infer that
\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}II_{3}(m)=0
. Hence passing to the limit in (
4.96
) we get
\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}I_{1}(m)=0
. In a similar fashion, we can also prove that
\lim_{m\longrightarrow\infty}I_{2}(m)=0
. Therefore, passing to the limit in (
4.94
), we obtain the convergence (
4.93
) and completes the proof of the almost surely convergence (
4.92
).

To finish the proof of equality (
4.91
), it remains to prove the uniform integrability of
R_{m}(t,s)
. For this purpose, using the Young inequality, a similar calculations as in inequality (
4.95
) and the estimates (
4.67
) and (
4.69
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left|R_{m}(t,s)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=1}^{3}\left|h_{i}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\left(G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1},G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}dr\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}(t-s)\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\int_{s}^{t}\left|G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1}\right|_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}\left|G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right|_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}dr

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{T}\left|G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1}\right|_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{4}dr+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{T}\left|G_{m}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right|_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{4}dr

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{T}\left|g(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))\right|^{4}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U},H)}dr+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\phi(\bar{c}_{m}(r))\right|^{4}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2},L^{2})}dr

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\sup_{0\leq r\leq T}(1+\left|(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m}(r),\bar{c}_{m}(r))\right|^{4}_{\mathcal{H}})+\mathcal{K}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\sup_{0\leq r\leq T}\left|\nabla\bar{c}_{m}(r))\right|^{4}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K},

which prove the uniform integrability of
R_{m}(t,s)
. Thus, invoking the Vitali Theorem, we obtain the convergence (
4.91
).

Taking into account the convergences (
4.89
), (
4.90
) and (
4.91
), we can pass to the limit in the equalities (
4.86
) and (
4.87
) to get

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(LN(t)-LN(s),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}h_{1}(\mathbf{u}|_{[0,s]})h_{2}(c|_{[0,s]})h_{3}(n|_{[0,s]})\right]=0,

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left(LN(t),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\left(LN(t),\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}-\left(LN(s),\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\left(LN(s),\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{H\times L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-\int_{0}^{t}\left(G(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{1},G(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))^{*}\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}ds\right)h_{1}(\mathbf{u}|_{[0,s]})h_{2}(c|_{[0,s]})h_{3}(n|_{[0,s]})\right]=0,

which complete the proof of Lemma
4.17
.
∎

Thanks to Lemma
4.17
, we apply the usual martingale representation theorem proved in
[
12
, Theorem 8.2]
to the process
LN
and conclude that there exists a probability space
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
, a filtration
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
and a
\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
-cylindrical Wiener process
\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{s}:=(\bar{W}_{s},\bar{\beta}_{s})
defined on the probability space
(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}})=(\Omega^{\prime}\times\tilde{\Omega},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\otimes\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\otimes\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
adapted to the filtration
\bar{\mathbb{F}}=\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\otimes\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
such that

LN(t,\omega^{\prime},\tilde{\omega})=\int_{0}^{t}G(\mathbf{u}(s,\omega^{\prime},\tilde{\omega}),c(s,\omega^{\prime},\tilde{\omega}))d\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{s}(\omega^{\prime},\tilde{\omega}),\qquad t\in[0,T],\qquad(\omega^{\prime},\tilde{\omega})\in\bar{\Omega},

where

LN(t,\omega^{\prime},\tilde{\omega})=LN(t,\omega^{\prime}),\ (\mathbf{u}(s,\omega^{\prime},\tilde{\omega}),c(s,\omega^{\prime},\tilde{\omega}))=(\mathbf{u}(s,\omega^{\prime}),c(s,\omega^{\prime})),\ t\in[0,T],\ (\omega^{\prime},\tilde{\omega})\in\bar{\Omega}.

This implies that in the probability space
(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}})
, for
t\in[0,T]
and
\bar{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.

(4.97)

\begin{cases}L^{1}N^{1}(t)=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}L^{1}g(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))d\bar{W}_{s},\text{ in }H,\vskip 5.69046pt\\
L^{2}N^{2}(t)=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}L^{2}\phi(c(s))d\bar{\beta}_{s},\text{ in }L^{2}(\mathcal{O}).\end{cases}

Thanks to (
2.12
) and (
4.70
) the estimate

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|g(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U},V^{*})}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|g(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U},H)}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left(1+\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))\right|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\right)<\infty,

and

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\phi(c(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2},H^{-2})}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\phi(c(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2},L^{2})}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}\left(1+\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|c(s)\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)<\infty,

yield that
L^{1}N^{1}
and
L^{2}N^{2}
in (
4.97
) are continuous martingale in
H
and
L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
respectively. In a similar fashion as in
[
6
, Proof of Theorem 1.1]
, using the continuity of the operators
L^{1}
and
L^{2}
, we get

\int_{0}^{t}L^{1}g(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))d\bar{W}_{s}=L^{1}\left(\int_{0}^{t}g(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))d\bar{W}_{s}\right)\text{ and }\int_{0}^{t}L^{2}\phi(c(s))d\bar{\beta}_{s}=L^{2}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\phi(c(s))d\bar{\beta}_{s}\right),

for all
t\in[0,T]
. Combining these two last inequalities with the injectivity of the operators
L^{1}
and
L^{2}
, we infer from the system (
4.97
) that for
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.98)

\begin{cases}N^{1}(t)=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}g(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))d\bar{W}_{s},\text{ in }V^{*},\vskip 5.69046pt\\
N^{2}(t)=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\phi(c(s))d\bar{\beta}_{s},\text{ in }H^{-2}(\mathcal{O}).\end{cases}

On the new probability space
(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}})
, we also extend the random variable
n(t)
by

n(t,\omega^{\prime},\tilde{\omega})=n(t,\omega^{\prime}),\ t\in[0,T],\ (\omega^{\prime},\tilde{\omega})\in\bar{\Omega},

and infer that the equality (
4.83
) also hods in
(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}})
. Using this, the definition of
N^{1}
and
N^{2}
, and the system (
4.98
), we derive that
(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}},(\mathbf{u},c,n),(\bar{W},\bar{\beta}))
satisfies the system (
3.2
). In particular, we have for all
t\in[0,T]
and
\bar{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.

\begin{cases}\mathbf{u}(t)=\mathbf{u}_{0}-\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}[\eta A_{0}\mathbf{u}(s)+B_{0}(\mathbf{u}(s),\mathbf{u}(s))+R_{0}(n(s),\varPhi)]ds+\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}g(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))d\bar{W}_{s},\text{ in }V^{*},\vskip 5.69046pt\\
c(t)=c_{0}-\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}[\xi A_{1}c(s)+B_{1}(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))-R_{1}(n(s),c(s))]ds+\gamma\int_{0}^{t}\phi(c(s))d\bar{\beta}_{s},\text{ in }H^{-2}(\mathcal{O}),\end{cases}

which can be written as

\begin{cases}\mathbf{u}(t)=\mathbf{u}_{0}-\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}G_{0}(s)ds+\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}S_{0}(s)d\bar{W}_{s},\text{ in }V^{*},\vskip 5.69046pt\\
c(t)=c_{0}-\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}G_{1}(s)ds+\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}S_{1}(s)d\bar{\beta}_{s},\text{ in }H^{-2}(\mathcal{O}),\end{cases}

where for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\begin{split}&G_{0}(t):=\eta A_{0}\mathbf{u}(t)+B_{0}(\mathbf{u}(t),\mathbf{u}(t))+R_{0}(n(t),\varPhi),\\
&G_{1}(t):=\xi A_{1}c(t)+B_{1}(\mathbf{u}(t),c(t))-R_{1}(n(t),c(t)),\\
&S_{0}(t):=g(\mathbf{u}(t),c(t)),\quad\text{and}\quad S_{1}(t):=\gamma\phi(c(t)).\end{split}

Since the identities (
4.66
), (
4.70
) and (
4.71
) hold, following the idea of the proof of estimate (
4.57
), we can see that
G_{0}\in L^{2}([0,T]\times\bar{\Omega};V^{*})
,
G_{1}\in L^{2}([0,T]\times\bar{\Omega};L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))
,
S_{0}\in L^{2}([0,T]\times\bar{\Omega};H)
and
S_{1}\in L^{2}([0,T]\times\bar{\Omega};H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))
. Therefore, it follows from
[
23
, Theorem 3.2]
that there exists
\bar{\Omega}_{0}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}
such that
\bar{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{\Omega}_{0})=1
and for all
\omega\in\bar{\Omega}_{0}
, the function
\mathbf{u}
and
c
take values in
H
and in
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})
respectively and are continuous in
H
and
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})
with respect to
t
.
Owing to the fact that
(\mathbf{u},c,n)
is
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{u}}\times\mathcal{Z}_{c}\times\mathcal{Z}_{n}
-valued random variable and progressively measurable over the filtration
\bar{\mathbb{F}}
, we derive that
(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}},(\mathbf{u},c,n),(\bar{W},\bar{\beta}))
is a probabilistic weak solution of system (
1.2
). We recall that the inequalities (
3.1
) directly follows from relations (
4.66
), (
4.70
), and (
4.71
).

5.
Properties of solution and energy inequality

In this section we prove the mass conservation property, the non-negativity property and the
L^{\infty}
-norm stability for the prrobabilistic strong solution of system (
1.2
). By these properties, we also prove an energy inequality which may be useful for the study of the invariant measure of system (
1.2
) which is still an opened problem according to our knowledge.

5.1.
Non-negativity and mass conservation

The following theorem gives the conservation of the total mass property and the non-negativity of the strong solutions of system (
1.2
).

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{A}=(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]},\mathbb{P})
be a filtered probability space,
\mathcal{U}
be a separable Hilbert space,
W
be
cylindrical Wiener process on
\mathcal{U}
over
\mathfrak{A}
, and
\beta=(\beta^{1},\beta^{2})
be a two dimensional standard Brownian motion
over
\mathfrak{A}
independent of
W
. If
(\mathbf{u},c,n)
is a probabilistic strong solution of system (
1.2
), then the following equality holds for all
t\in[0,T]

(5.1)

\int_{\mathcal{O}}n(t,x)dx=\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{0}(x)dx,\ \mathbb{P}\text{-a.s}.

Furthermore, if
c_{0}>0
and
n_{0}>0
, then the following inequality hold
\mathbb{P}
-a.s

(5.2)

n(t)>0,\text{ and }c(t)>0,\text{ for all }t\in[0,T].

Proof.

We Note that, the conservation of the total mass (
5.1
) follows straightforwardly
from the fact that
\nabla\cdot\mathbf{u}=0
and the proof of (
5.2
) is very similar to the proof of Lemma
3.6
.
∎

The following theorem gives the
L^{\infty}
-stability of the probabilistic strong solution of system (
1.2
).

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{A}=(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]},\mathbb{P})
be a filtered probability space,
\mathcal{U}
be a separable Hilbert space,
W
be
cylindrical Wiener process on
\mathcal{U}
over
\mathfrak{A}
, and
\beta=(\beta^{1},\beta^{2})
be a two dimensional standard Brownian motion
over
\mathfrak{A}
independent of
W
. If
(\mathbf{u},c,n)
is a probabilistic strong solution of system (
1.2
) in the filtered probability space
\mathfrak{A}
, then for all
t\in[0,T]

(5.3)

\left|c(t)\right|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}},\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of Corollary
3.7
.
∎

5.2.
Energy inequality

In this subsection, we will derive an energy inequality. The probabilistic strong solution
(\mathbf{u},n,c)
involving the following Lyapunov functional

\mathcal{E}(n,c,\mathbf{u})(t)=\int_{\mathcal{O}}n(t)\ln n(t)dx+\mathcal{K}_{f}\left|\nabla c(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{8\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi\eta}\left|\mathbf{u}(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+e^{-1}\left|\mathcal{O}\right|,\quad t\in[0,T],

where
\mathcal{K}_{GN}
is a constant given by the Gagliardo-Niremberg inequality (
3.7
) and
\mathcal{K}_{f}
is defined in (
2.2
).

Proposition 5.3
.

Suppose that Assumption 1, Assumption 2 and the following inequality

(5.4)

\frac{4\mathcal{K}_{f}\displaystyle\max_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{2}}{\displaystyle\min_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{\prime}}\leq\delta,

are satisfied. Let
\mathfrak{A}=(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]},\mathbb{P})
be a filtered probability space,
\mathcal{U}
be a separable Hilbert space,
W
be
cylindrical Wiener process on
\mathcal{U}
over
\mathfrak{A}
, and
\beta=(\beta^{1},\beta^{2})
be a two dimensional standard Brownian motion
over
\mathfrak{A}
independent of
W
. Then, any probabilistic strong solution
(\mathbf{u},c,n)
of system (
1.2
) in the filtered probability space
\mathfrak{A}
satisfies the following entropy functional relations for almost all
t\in[0,T]
,

(5.5)

\displaystyle\left|c(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\eta\int_{0}^{t}\left|\nabla c(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+2\int_{0}^{t}(n(s)f(c(s)),c(s))ds=\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(n,c,\mathbf{u})(t)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\left[\delta\left|\nabla\sqrt{n(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{3\xi\mathcal{K}_{f}}{2}\left|\Delta c(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{8\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left|\sqrt{n(s)}\nabla c(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{E}(n_{0},c_{0},\mathbf{u}_{0})+\mathcal{K}_{5}t+\mathcal{K}_{6}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\mathbf{u}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\gamma^{2}\mathcal{K}_{f}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\nabla\phi(c(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}ds

(5.6)

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{8\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi\eta}\int_{0}^{t}\left|g(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U};H)}ds+2\gamma\mathcal{K}_{f}\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla\phi(c(s)),\nabla c(s))d\beta_{s}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{16\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi\eta}\int_{0}^{t}(g(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s)),\mathbf{u}(s))dWs,

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,
where
\mathcal{K}_{5}
and
\mathcal{K}_{6}
are some positive constant to be given later.

Proof.

The equality (
5.5
) follows directly from the application of the Itô formula to
t\mapsto\left|c(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}
and the fact that

(B_{1}(\mathbf{u},c),c)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{u}(x)\cdot\nabla c^{2}(x)dx=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}c^{2}(x)\nabla\cdot\mathbf{u}(x)dx=0,

as well as

(\phi(c),c)=\sum_{k=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\sigma_{k}(x)\cdot\nabla c(x)c(x)dx=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\sigma_{k}(x)\cdot\nabla c^{2}(x)dx=0

and

\left|\phi(c)\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}=\left|\nabla c\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Next, we multiply equation (
2.14
)
3
by
1+\ln n(s)
for
s\in[0,t]
and integrate the resulting equation in
\mathcal{O}
to obtain

(5.7)

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathcal{O}}n(s,x)\ln n(s,x)dx+\delta\int_{\mathcal{O}}\dfrac{\left|\nabla n(s,x)\right|^{2}}{n(s,x)}dx=\chi\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla n(s,x)\cdot\nabla c(s,x)dx.

Thanks to the Young inequality and the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality we note that

\chi\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla n(x)\cdot\nabla c(x)dx\leq 2\delta\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n(x)}\right|^{2}dx+\frac{\chi^{2}}{2\delta}\int_{\mathcal{O}}n(x)\left|\nabla c(x)\right|^{2}dx.

Combining the last inequality with equality (
5.7
) we arrive at

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}}n(t,x)\ln n(t,x)dx+2\delta\int_{0}^{t}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{0}(x)\ln n_{0}(x)dx

(5.8)

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{\chi^{2}}{2\delta}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{n(s)}\nabla c(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds.

By applying the Itô formula to
t\mapsto\left|\nabla c(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}
, we find that

\displaystyle\left|\nabla c(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\xi\int_{0}^{t}\left|\Delta c(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle=\left|\nabla c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}-2\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla B_{1}(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s)),\nabla c(s))ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}-2\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla R_{2}(n(s),c(s)),\nabla c(s))ds

(5.9)

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\gamma^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\nabla\phi(c(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}+2\gamma\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla\phi(c(s)),\nabla c(s))d\beta_{s}.

Due to the Assumption
1
and the
L^{\infty}
-norm stability obtained in Theorem
5.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle(\nabla B_{1}(\mathbf{u},c),\nabla c)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla c\right|^{2}_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\xi}{16\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}\left|\nabla c\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\frac{4\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\xi}{4}\left|\Delta c\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{4\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\xi(4\mathcal{K}_{2}+3)}{16}\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}.

and

\displaystyle-(\nabla R_{2}(n,c),\nabla c(s))ds

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\displaystyle\min_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{\prime}(c)}{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}n(x)\left|\nabla c(x)\right|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{1}{2\displaystyle\min_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{\prime}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}f^{2}(c(x))\frac{\left|\nabla n(x)\right|^{2}}{n(x)}dx

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\displaystyle\min_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{\prime}(c)}{2}\left|\sqrt{n}\nabla c\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{2\displaystyle\max_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{2}}{\displaystyle\min_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{\prime}(c)}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Thus, we see from (
5.9
) that

\displaystyle\left|\nabla c(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{3\xi}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\Delta c(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\displaystyle\min_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{(s)}\nabla c(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\left|\nabla c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\xi(4\mathcal{K}_{2}+3)}{8}\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}t+\frac{8\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{4\displaystyle\max_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{2}}{\displaystyle\min_{0\leq c\leq\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}}f^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n(s)}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\gamma^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\nabla\phi(c(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}ds+2\gamma\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla\phi(c(s)),\nabla c(s))d\beta_{s}.

Now, we multiply this last inequality by
\mathcal{K}_{f}
, add the result with inequality (
5.2
), and use the inequality (
5.4
) to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}}n(t,x)\ln n(t,x)dx

\displaystyle+\mathcal{K}_{f}\left|\nabla c(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{3\xi\mathcal{K}_{f}}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\Delta c(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+2\delta\int_{0}^{t}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n(s)}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{n(s)}\nabla c(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}_{f}\left|\nabla c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{\mathcal{O}}n_{0}(x)\ln n_{0}(x)dx+\frac{\mathcal{K}_{f}\xi(4\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathcal{K}_{2}+3)}{8}\left|c_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}t

(5.10)

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{8\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\gamma^{2}\mathcal{K}_{f}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\nabla\phi(c(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};L^{2})}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+2\gamma\mathcal{K}_{f}\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla\phi(c(s)),\nabla c(s))d\beta_{s}.

Using the equality (
5.1
) and the inequality (
3.7
) we note that

\displaystyle\left|n\right|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left(\left|\sqrt{n}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n}\right|_{L^{2}}+\left|\sqrt{n}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)

(5.11)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left(\left|n_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n}\right|_{L^{2}}+\left|n_{0}\right|_{L^{1}}\right),

which altogether with the Itô formula to
t\mapsto\left|\mathbf{u}(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}
implies the existence of
\mathcal{K}_{3}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{u}(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\eta\int_{0}^{t}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left|\nabla\Phi\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|n(s)\right|_{L^{2}}\left|\mathbf{u}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|g(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U};H)}ds+2\int_{0}^{t}(g(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s)),\mathbf{u}(s))dWs

(5.12)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\delta\eta}{\mathcal{K}_{4}}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\nabla\sqrt{n(s)}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds+\mathcal{K}_{3}\left|\nabla\Phi\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\left|n_{0}\right|_{L^{1}}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\mathbf{u}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{1}{2}t+\frac{1}{2}\left|\nabla\Phi\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\left|n_{0}\right|_{L^{1}}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\mathbf{u}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|g(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s))\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U};H)}ds+2\int_{0}^{t}(g(\mathbf{u}(s),c(s)),\mathbf{u}(s))dWs,

with
\mathcal{K}_{4}=\frac{8\mathcal{K}_{f}\mathcal{K}_{GN}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}}{3\xi}
. Multiplying the inequality (
5.12
) by
\frac{\mathcal{K}_{4}}{\eta}
, and adding the result with inequality (
5.10
), we obtain some positive constants
\mathcal{K}_{5}
and
\mathcal{K}_{6}
such that the inequality (
5.6
) holds.
∎

Appendix A
Compactness and tightness criteria

In this appendix we recall several compactness and tightness criteria that are frequently used in this paper.

We start with the following lemma based on the Dubinsky Theorem.

Lemma A.1
.

Let us consider the space

(A.1)

\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}=L_{w}^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))\cap L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];H^{-3}(\mathcal{O}))

and
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}
be the supremum of the corresponding topologies. Then a set
\bar{\bar{K}}_{0}\subset\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}
is
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}
-relatively compact if the following three conditions hold

(a)

\sup\limits_{\varphi\in\bar{\bar{K}}_{0}}\displaystyle\int\limits_{0}^{T}|\varphi(s)|_{H^{1}}^{2}ds<\infty
, i.e.,
\bar{\bar{K}}_{0}
is bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))
,

(b)

\exists\gamma>0
:
\sup\limits_{\varphi\in\bar{\bar{K}}_{0}}\left|\varphi\right|_{C^{\gamma}([0,T];H^{-3})}<\infty
.

Proof.

We note that the following embedding is continuous
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow H^{-3}(\mathcal{O})
with
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
compact. By the Banach-Alaoglu
Theorem condition (a) yields that
\bar{\bar{K}}_{0}
is compact in
L_{w}^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))
. Moreover (b) implies that the functions
\varphi\in\bar{\bar{K}}_{0}
are equicontinuous, i.e. for all
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\delta>0
such that if
\left|t-s\right|<\delta
then
\left|\varphi(t)-\varphi(s)\right|_{H^{-3}}<\varepsilon
for all
\varphi\in\bar{\bar{K}}_{0}
. We can then apply Dubinsky’s Theorem (see
[
41
, Theorem IV.4.1]
) since by condition (a),
\bar{\bar{K}}_{0}
is bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))
.
∎

Following the same method as in
[
8
, Lemma 3.3 ]
, we obtain the following compactness result.

Lemma A.2
.

Let us consider the space

(A.2)

\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{n}=L_{w}^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))\cap L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];H^{-3}(\mathcal{O}))\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];L^{2}_{w}(\mathcal{O})),

and
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}
be the supremum of the corresponding topologies. Then a set
\bar{\bar{K}}_{0}\subset\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{n}
is
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}
-relatively compact if the following three conditions hold

(a)

\sup\limits_{\varphi\in\bar{\bar{K}}_{0}}\left|\varphi\right|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2})}<\infty
,

(b)

\sup\limits_{\varphi\in\bar{\bar{K}}_{0}}\displaystyle\int\limits_{0}^{T}|\varphi(s)|_{H^{1}}^{2}ds<\infty
, i.e.,
\bar{\bar{K}}_{0}
is bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))
,

(c)

\exists\gamma>0
:
\sup\limits_{\varphi\in\bar{\bar{K}}_{0}}\left|\varphi\right|_{C^{\gamma}([0,T];H^{-3})}<\infty
.

From this lemma we also get the following tightness criteria for stochastic processes with paths in
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{n}
where the proof is the same as the proof of
[
3
, Lemma 5.5]
.

Lemma A.3

(Tightness criterion for
n
)
.

Let
\gamma>0
be a given parameters and
(\varphi_{n})_{n}
be a sequence of continuous
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
-adapted
H^{-3}(\mathcal{O})
-valued processes. Let
\mathcal{L}_{m}
be the law of
\varphi_{n}
on
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{n}
. If for any
\varepsilon>0
there exists a constant
\mathcal{K}_{i}
,
i=1,...,3
such that

\begin{split}&\sup_{m}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\varphi_{m}\right|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2})}>K_{1}\right)\leq\varepsilon,\\
&\sup_{m}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\varphi_{m}\right|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1})}>K_{2}\right)\leq\varepsilon,\\
&\sup_{m}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\varphi_{m}\right|_{C^{\gamma}(0,T;H^{-3})}>K_{3}\right)\leq\varepsilon,\end{split}

then the sequence
(\mathcal{L}_{m})_{m}
is tight on
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{n}
.

The following compactness results are due to
[
7
, Theorem 4.4 and Theorem 4.5]
(see also
[
28
]
), where we can see the details of the proof.

Lemma A.4
.

Let us consider the space

(A.3)

\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{u}}=L_{w}^{2}(0,T;V)\cap L^{2}(0,T;H)\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];V^{*})\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];H_{w}),

and
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{1}
be the supremum of the corresponding topologies. Then a set
\bar{\bar{K}}_{1}\subset\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{u}}
is
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{1}
-relatively compact if the following three conditions hold

(a)

\sup\limits_{\mathbf{v}\in\bar{\bar{K}}_{1}}\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}|\mathbf{v}(t)|_{L^{2}}<\infty
,

(b)

\sup\limits_{\mathbf{v}\in\bar{\bar{K}}_{1}}\displaystyle\int\limits_{0}^{T}|\nabla\mathbf{v}(s)|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds<\infty
, i.e.,
\bar{\bar{K}}_{2}
is bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;V)
,

(c)

\lim\limits_{\delta\to 0}\sup\limits_{\mathbf{v}\in\bar{\bar{K}}_{1}}\sup\limits_{s,t\in[0,T],|t-s|\leq\delta}\left|\mathbf{v}(t)-\mathbf{v}(s)\right|_{V^{*}}=0
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let us consider the space

(A.4)

\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{c}=L_{w}^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\mathcal{O}))\cap L^{2}(0,T;H_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{O}))\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))\cap\mathcal{C}([0,T];H_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{O})),

and
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{2}
be the supremum of the corresponding topologies. Then a set
\bar{\bar{K}}_{2}\subset\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{c}
is
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{2}
-relatively compact if the following three conditions hold

(a)

\sup\limits_{\varphi\in\bar{\bar{K}}_{2}}\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}|\varphi(t)|_{H^{1}}<\infty
,

(b)

\sup\limits_{\varphi\in\bar{\bar{K}}_{2}}\displaystyle\int\limits_{0}^{T}|\varphi(s)|_{H^{2}}^{2}ds<\infty
, i.e.,
\bar{\bar{K}}_{2}
is bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\mathcal{O}))
,

(c)

\lim\limits_{\delta\to 0}\sup\limits_{\varphi\in\bar{\bar{K}}_{2}}\sup\limits_{s,t\in[0,T],|t-s|\leq\delta}\left|\varphi(t)-\varphi(s)\right|_{L^{2}}=0
.

We now consider a filtered probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
with filtration
\mathbb{F}:=\left\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
satisfying the usual hypotheses. Let
(\mathbb{M},d_{1})
be a complete, separable metric space and

(y_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of
\mathbb{F}
-adapted and
\mathbb{M}
-valued processes. We recall from
[
20
]
the following definition.

Definition A.6
.

A sequence
(y_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies the
Aldous condition
in the space
\mathbb{M}
if and only if

\displaystyle\forall\epsilon>0\penalty\ \forall\zeta>0\penalty\ \exists\delta>0\penalty\ \text{such that for every sequence}\penalty\ (\tau_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\penalty\ \text{of}\penalty\ \mathbb{F}\text{-}\text{stopping times with}

\displaystyle\tau_{n}\leq T\penalty\ \text{one has}\penalty\ \sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{0\leq\theta\leq\delta}\mathbb{P}\left\{|y_{n}(\tau_{n}+\theta)-y_{n}(\tau_{n})|_{\mathbb{M}}\geq\zeta\right\}\leq\epsilon.

In Definition
A.6
, and throughout we understand that
y_{n}
is extended to zero outside the interval
[0,T]
.

The following lemma is proved in
[
28
, Appendix A, Lemma 6.3]
.

Lemma A.7
.

Let
(X,|.|_{X})
be a separable Banach space and let
(y_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of
X
-valued random variables. Assume that for every
(\tau_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\mathbb{F}
-stoppings times with
\tau_{n}\leq T
and for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\theta\geq 0
the following condition holds

(A.5)

\mathbb{E}\left|y_{n}(\tau_{n}+\theta)-y_{n}(\tau_{n})\right|_{X}^{\alpha}\leq C\theta^{\beta},

for some
\alpha,\beta>0
and some constant
C>0
. Then the sequence
(y_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies the
Aldous condition
in the space
X
.

In the view of Lemma
A.4
and Lemma
4.2
, in the next corollaries, we will state a tightness criteria for stochastic processes with part in
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{u}}
or in
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{c}
.

Corollary A.8
.

Let
(\mathbf{v}_{m})_{m}
be a sequence of continuous
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
-adapted
V^{*}
-valued processes satisfying

(a):

there exists a constant
\mathcal{K}_{1}>0
such that

\sup_{m}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\mathcal{K}_{1},

(b):

there exists a constant
\mathcal{K}_{2}>0
such that

\sup_{m}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla\mathbf{v}_{m}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\leq\mathcal{K}_{2},

(c):

(\mathbf{v}_{m})_{m}
satisfies the
Aldous condition
in
V^{*}
.

Let
\mathcal{L}_{m}(\mathbf{v}_{m})
be the law of
\mathbf{v}_{m}
on
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{u}}
. Then, the sequence
(\mathcal{L}_{m}(\mathbf{v}_{m}))_{m}
is tight in
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{u}}
.

Corollary A.9
.

(v_{m})_{m}
be a sequence of continuous
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
-adapted
L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
-valued processes satisfying

(a):

there exists a constant
\mathcal{K}_{1}>0
such that

\sup_{m}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|v_{m}(s)\right|_{H^{1}}^{2}\leq\mathcal{K}_{1},

(b):

there exists a constant
\mathcal{K}_{2}>0
such that

\sup_{m}\int_{0}^{T}\left|v_{m}(s)\right|_{H^{2}}^{2}ds\leq\mathcal{K}_{2},

(c):

(v_{m})_{m}
satisfies the
Aldous condition
in
L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
.

Let
\mathcal{L}_{m}(v_{m})
be the law of
v_{m}
on
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{c}
. Then, the sequence
(\mathcal{L}_{m}(v_{m}))_{m}
is tight in
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{c}
.
